of the financial obligations of the district at the end of the report year, including
the amount of outstanding indebtedness, the amount and terms of any new district indebtedness
or long-term obligations issued in the report year, the amount of payment or retirement
of existing indebtedness of the district in the report year, the total assessed valuation
of all taxable properties within the district as of January 1 of the report year,
and the current mill levy of the district pledged to debt retirement in the report
year.
5.
The district's budget for the calendar year in which the annual report is submitted.
6.
A summary of mixed-use residential and commercial development (whichever is appropriate)
which has occurred within the district for the report year.
7.
A summary of all fees, charges and assessments imposed by the district as of January
1 of the report year.
8.
Certification by the board that no action, event or condition enumerated in section 4.10.090 of these policies has occurred in the report year.
9.
The name, business address and telephone number of each member of the board and its
chief administrative officer and general counsel, together with the date, place and
time of the regular meetings of the board.
B.
In the event the annual report is not received by the city clerk on a timely basis,
notice of such default shall be given by the city clerk to the board of such district,
at its last known address. The failure of the district to file the annual report within
30 days of the mailing of such default notice by the city clerk shall empower the
city council to impose the sanctions authorized in this chapter. The remedies provided
for noncompliance with the filing of the annual report shall be supplemental to the
remedy authorized under C.R.S. § 32-1-209.
(Code 1993, § 4.10.080; Code 2009, § 4.10.080; Ord. No. O-2005-46, § 1; Ord. No. O-2019-13 , § 2, 2-12-2019; Ord. No. O-2020-11 , § 2, 8-11-2020)
A.
The city council will determine whether a change to the service plan constitutes a
material modification of the service plan. The examples listed below include, but
are not limited to, what constitutes a material modification. The occurrence of any
of the following actions, events or conditions subsequent to the date of approval
of the service plan or most recent amendment thereto are presumed to be changes of
a basic or essential nature of a district, requiring a service plan amendment.
1.
Increase of mill levy beyond existing caps, increase of bond authorization, increase
in level of bonded indebtedness beyond bond authorization.
2.
Any change of the district boundaries.
3.
Default in the payment of principal or interest of any district bonds, notes, certificates,
debentures, contracts or other evidences of indebtedness or borrowing issued or incurred
by the district.
4.
Initiating a proceeding for debt adjustment or the confirmation of a plan for adjustment
of debt under chapter 9 of the bankruptcy code.
5.
Failure of the district to develop any capital facility proposed in its service plan
when necessary to service approved development within the district.
6.
The occurrence of any event or condition, which is defined under the service plan
or any intergovernmental agreement as necessitating a service plan amendment.
7.
Default by the district under any intergovernmental agreement with the city or any
other government.
8.
The creation of any subdistrict.
9.
Any of the events or conditions enumerated in section 32-1-207(2) of the Special District
Act.
B.
If a material modification has taken place, the district shall submit its request
for an amendment in accordance with these policies. If the city council finds that
no material modification has taken place, the district shall be relieved from obtaining
an amendment for the events or occurrences reviewed by the city council. The city
council retains the right to require an amendment thereafter if the change or deviation,
on a cumulative basis, subsequently becomes material. In making its determination,
the city council shall consider, among other relevant information, whether the modification
will have a probable adverse financial impact on the city.
(Code 1993, § 4.10.090; Code 2009, § 4.10.090; Ord. No. O-2005-46, § 1)
A.
Within 90 days of the occurrence of an action, event or condition enumerated above
in the section on material modifications, the board shall forward an appropriate petition
to the city council for approval requesting a service plan amendment (amendment).
The petition for amendment shall include the following information:
1.
Any information or documentation required under the applicable provisions of the Special
District Act or this Code.
2.
Any changes to any of the information, assumptions or projects furnished in conjunction
with the petition for approval of organization of a district or contained in the service
plan since the service plan was last reviewed and approved by the city council.
3.
A detailed explanation of the activity, events or conditions which resulted in the
material modification to the service plan, including what action was taken or alternatives
considered, if any, by the district to avoid the action, event or condition.
4.
The impact of the material modification on the district's ability to develop the capital
facilities and infrastructure necessary to meet its capital development plan.
5.
The effect of the material modification on the district's ability to retire its outstanding
financial obligations as scheduled and its ability to issue and market additional
indebtedness to finance additional capital expenditures.
6.
A current financial plan for the district reflecting development absorption rates
anticipated within the district's service area, projected annual revenues and expenditures
based upon such projected absorption rates, debt issuance and amortization schedules,
and a projection of anticipated capital outlays.
7.
The financial impact of the modification on existing residents of the district.
8.
An updated five-year capital improvements plan.
9.
Alternatives or options available to the district if the requested amendment is not
approved.
B.
All of the required information shall be supported by appropriate technical analysis,
reports and supporting documents of qualified professionals and consultants. The amendment
shall be processed and reviewed in the same manner as prescribed by these policies
for an initial service plan, except that the submittal requirements of this section
shall be substituted for those of the section titled service plan contents. The application
fee shall remain at $1,000.00. This section shall not impair the right of the city
to bring an action in the district court to enjoin the activities of the district
pursuant to section 32-l-207(3)(b) of the Special District Act.
(Code 1993, § 4.10.100; Code 2009, § 4.10.100; Ord. No. O-2005-46, § 1)
Any quinquennial (five-year) review required under C.R.S. § 32-1-1101 shall be subject
to the following procedures:
A.
Five copies of the application shall be submitted to the city manager together with
an application fee of $1,000.00.
B.
Within 60 days of receipt of a complete application, the city manager shall submit
a report to the city council including a recommendation as to the statutory findings
that should be made by the city council on the application.
C.
The application shall be reviewed and considered at a regular meeting of the city
council held within 60 days of the submission of the report of the city manager.
D.
The city council shall consider all evidence and testimony, as it deems relevant to
its consideration of the application.
E.
A written decision on the application shall be made by resolution of the city council
within 20 days of conclusion of its review and consideration of the application.
(Code 1993, § 4.10.110; Code 2009, § 4.10.110; Ord. No. O-2005-46, § 1)
The district shall not claim any entitlement to monies from the state conservation
trust fund. The district shall remit to the city all monies it may receive from this
fund.
(Code 1993, § 4.10.120; Code 2009, § 4.10.120; Ord. No. O-2005-46, § 1)
A.
In the event there is reason to believe that the purposes for which the district was
created have been accomplished, a public hearing shall be conducted before the city
council to determine whether the district should be dissolved. Prior written notice
of such hearing shall be provided to the board of directors of the district. Upon
an independent determination of the city council that the purposes for which the district
was created have been accomplished, the district shall agree to file a petition in
district court for dissolution. In any event, such dissolution shall not occur until
the district has provided for the payment or discharge of all of its outstanding indebtedness
and other financial obligations.
B.
The district shall not file a request with the district court to consolidate with
another title 32 district without the prior written approval of the city council.
(Code 1993, § 4.10.130; Code 2009, § 4.10.130; Ord. No. O-2005-46, § 1)
Should any district fail to request and obtain a service plan amendment when required
under the terms of these policies or otherwise fail to fully and completely comply
with these policies, the city council by resolution may impose any of the following
sanctions, as it deems appropriate:
A.
Exercise any applicable remedy under the Special District Act.
B.
Exercise any legal remedy under the terms of any intergovernmental agreement under
which the district is in default.
C.
Exercise any other legal remedy, including seeking injunctive relief against the district,
to force compliance with the provisions of this chapter.
(Code 1993, § 4.10.140; Code 2009, § 4.10.140; Ord. No. O-2005-46, § 1)
A.
If any district has not undertaken development of capital facilities or issued any
indebtedness, it may apply to the city for an exemption from compliance with these
policies.
1.
The city council shall grant an exemption if the board submits a resolution to the
city council stating that upon issuance of the exemption, the district's authorization
under the service plan and the intergovernmental agreement with the city to undertake
development of capital facilities or issue any indebtedness is suspended.
2.
With issuance of the exemption, the district shall be excluded from compliance with
these policies except that the district annually, not later than September 1, shall
submit financial statements from the previous year and the budget for the current
year.
B.
Prior to any district with an exempt status undertaking capital development or issuing
any indebtedness authorized under its service plan or the Special District Act other
than regulatory reporting, it shall fully comply with the provisions of these policies.
(Code 1993, § 4.10.150; Code 2009, § 4.10.150; Ord. No. O-2005-46, § 1)
I.
Introduction.
II.
Purpose of district.
III.
Proposed district boundaries/maps.
IV.
Proposed land use/population projections.
V.
Description of proposed services.
a.
Types of improvements
i.
Water.
ii.
Streets.
iii.
Others.
b.
Standards of construction/statement of compatibility.
c.
Facilities to be contracted and/or acquired.
VI.

VII.

Assessed valuation.

f.

XV.

XVI.
Annual report.
XVII.
Conclusion.
ATTACHMENTS
A.
Legal description of district boundaries
B.
District boundary map
C.
Vicinity map
D.
List of interested parties
E.
Matrix: Improvement, Built By, Donated to City of Longmont, Maintained By (District,
Developer, City, Etc.)

(1)
District landscape maintenance on dedicated city right-of-way will be permitted by
landscape maintenance easement from city.
A.
General. Special improvement districts, authorized by C.R.S. title 31, article 25, part 5
(C.R.S. § 31-25-501 et seq.), are typically formed to finance infrastructure improvements
within newly emerging urban areas. Such districts are authorized to issue tax-exempt
bonds to fund the installation of streets, sidewalks, water and sewer lines, street
lighting, and other public improvements. Bonds are paid with a property tax levied
on all property located in the district.
B.
Formation of district discretionary with city council. Consistent with the provisions of section 10.4 of the Charter for the city, nothing in this section and nothing in any general laws
of the state shall be deemed to impose any obligation upon the city council to create
any particular special improvement district. Such findings and the decision as to
whether any particular special improvement district shall or shall not be created
shall remain within the sole discretion of the city council. The exercise of that
discretion shall not be questioned in any action or proceeding.
C.
General law to control. Except as provided by the Charter and except as provided in this section, the procedure
for the creation of special or local improvement districts, the method and manner
of making improvements, the assessment of costs, and the issuance of bonds, shall
be as provided by the general laws of the state relating to special or local improvement
districts (C.R.S. title 31, article 25, part 5 (C.R.S. § 31-25-501 et seq.)).
D.
Types of improvements. The improvements authorized to be acquired, constructed, or installed in special
or local improvement districts may consist of the construction, reconstruction, repair,
replacement, and extension of improvements that result in a special benefit to the
real property included within the district. Without limiting the generality of the
foregoing, such improvements may include grading, paving, curbing, guttering, lighting
or otherwise improving any street or alley; traffic safety improvements; including
signals and signs; sidewalk and bike path improvements, park, open space and recreational
improvements; storm drainage and flood-control improvements; water, sewer, gas, electrical
and other utility improvements, and aesthetic improvements and amenities. Improvements
authorized by this section shall be located in or on public rights-of-way or other
public property, except as may be otherwise provided in the ordinance creating the
district.
E.
Method of assessment. In all cases where the cost of a local improvement is assessed wholly or in part
upon the real property within the district, the cost shall be assessed in proportion
to the special benefit received. Such assessment may be made in a frontage, area,
zone, unit or other equitable basis according to special benefits, as determined by
the city council. Two or more methods of assessments for different kinds of improvements
may be included in a single district.
F.
Waiver of hearing. If the petition for the creation of a special or local improvement district is signed
by 100 percent of the owners of the real property to be included within the district
and to be assessed, and contains a request for waiver of the notice, publication,
mailing and the hearing on the creation of the district, or if each of the property
owners otherwise executes a waiver agreement satisfactory to the city council, the
city council may, in its discretion, waive any or all of the requirements for notice,
publication, mailing and the hearing on the creation of the district.
G.
Application fee. All petitions shall be accompanied by an application review fee.
H.
Requirements relating to undeveloped real property.
1.
In the event that the proposed district consists of undeveloped real property, or
is substantially undeveloped, then prior to filing a petition and waiver described
in subsection F of this section, the following shall be required:
a.
Submission of the detailed set of plans and specifications showing the proposed improvements
to be consistent with official city land use and infrastructure plans and specifications;
b.
A title insurance policy or other evidence acceptable to the city council indicating
ownership of the real property in the name of the petitioner;
c.
In the petition and waiver referred to in subsection F of this section, inclusion
of a waiver of the right to contest or challenge the creation of the district, the
method and amount of assessments to be levied or imposed, and the issuance of bonds
for the payment of the costs and expenses of the improvements;
d.
Provisions to ensure the payment of special assessments to become due and payable
upon the sale of each lot or parcel to a third party; and
e.
An independent appraisal satisfactory to the city certifying the market value of the
real property to be included in the district, after the acquisition, construction
and installation of the improvements, will be at least two times the amount of the
proposed assessments.
2.
The city council may, by resolution or ordinance, waive or modify any of the above
requirements.
I.
Incontestability provision in bonds. Any ordinance that authorizes the issuance of special assessment bonds may provide
that each bond therein authorized shall recite that it is issued under the authority
of the Charter and the procedure ordinance codified in this section adopted under
to the Charter. Such recital shall conclusively impart full compliance with all of
the provisions thereof, and all bonds issued containing such recitals shall be incontestable
for any cause whatsoever after their delivery for value.
J.
Legal proceedings. No action or proceeding, at law or in equity, to review or to question the validity
or enjoin the performance of any act or the issuance or collection of any bonds or
the levy or collection of any assessments authorized herein, or for any other relief
against any acts or proceedings done or had under to this section or under the provisions
of the Charter, with reference hereto, whether based upon irregularities or jurisdictional
defects, shall be maintained unless commenced within 30 days after the performance
of the act or the passage of the resolution or ordinance complained of, and all such
actions or proceedings shall be thereafter perpetually barred.
K.
Costs of improvements. The total cost of the improvements that may be included in the assessment roll and
assessed against the property within the district includes all direct or incidental
costs, whether incurred or to be incurred, in connection with creation and administration
of the district, acquisition or construction of improvements, levying and collection
of assessments, issuance of bonds, and all other matters required or permitted by
this section. Without limiting the generality of the foregoing, such cost shall include
initiating and creating the district; publishing or posting notices; printing costs;
holding meetings and hearings; designing, constructing or acquiring improvements,
including acquiring land, easements and other property rights; contingencies and reserves;
inspection and collection; issuing and securing bonds, including capitalized interest
for such period as the city council may deem necessary, capitalized bond reserves,
and credit enhancements; levying and collecting assessments; legal, engineering, appraisal,
financial and other professional fees and expenses connected with the creation of
the district, the acquisition or construction of improvements, issuing or securing
bonds, levying and collecting assessments, or any other matter under this section,
including the costs of confirming, proving or defending the validity of any district,
bond or assessment; administrative costs; supplies and equipment; and all other incidental
costs.
(Code 2009, § 4.11.010; Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 4, 8-14-2018)
Editor's note— Ord. No. O-2011-25, adopted April 26, 2011, amended Ch. 4.12 in its entirety to read
as herein set out. Former Ch. 4.12 pertained to the same subject matter, consisted
of §§ 4.12.010—4.12.620, and derived from the 1993 Code; Ord. O-87-38; Ord. O-88-42;
Ord. O-93-05; Ord. O-94-11; Ord. O-94-50A; Ord. O-96-26; Ord. O-2005-60; and Ord.
No. O-2009-57, adopted Sept. 22, 2009.
The purpose of this chapter is to foster public confidence in governmental efficiency,
integrity and accountability, and to maximize the purchasing value of public funds
in procurement of goods and services best meeting the general and unique specifications
required in the operation of government. Nothing herein is intended to confer any
direct or implied right of enforcement to any person due to claim of loss of opportunity
or otherwise; however, this chapter will derivatively promote fair and equitable treatment
of all persons participating and competing in the purchasing processes of the city.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.010; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011)
Except as otherwise provided, this chapter applies to contracts for the procurement
of supplies, services and construction entered into by the city and its political
subdivisions after the effective date of the ordinance from which this chapter is
derived, unless the city and a contractor agree to its application to a contract entered
into prior to the effective date. It applies to every expenditure of public funds
by the city for public purchases irrespective of the source of the funds. When the
procurement involves federal, state or other grant assistance or the expenditure of
federal, state or other grant funds, the procurement will be conducted in accordance
with any mandatory applicable federal or state laws and regulations, or grant requirements.
Nothing in this chapter prevents the city from complying with the terms and conditions
of any grant, gift, bequest or cooperative agreement that is otherwise lawful. Except
for sections 4.12.370 through 4.12.390 and sections 4.12.450 through 4.12.530 , this chapter does not apply to:
1.
The following agreements approved by city council in open public meetings: intergovernmental
agreements requiring city council approval as provided in section 4.12.095 , economic development services with any entity to provide economic development services,
and Longmont Public Media or any successor to that entity for providing public, educational
and governmental cable television services to the residents of the city;
2.
Contracts for council-approved housing and human services, pursuant to section 2.58.030 ;
3.
Development and redevelopment agreements, under title 15 ;
4.
Franchise and other agreements for the use of the streets or public places or property
of the city in accordance with article XII of the Charter, subject to other applicable
laws and ordinances;
5.
Procurement of insurance services through competitive selection by insurance brokers
or consultants otherwise under contract with the city;
6.
Licenses and permits for telecommunications facilities on city sites and facilities,
pursuant to chapter 13.37 ;
7.
Art in public places agreements, under chapter 14.42 ;
8.
Grant awards to other persons as may be authorized by the city;
9.
Acquisition or disposal of real property, including land and/or buildings;
10.
Agreements brought before city council by the purchasing and contracts manager and
approved by city council in open public meetings; and
11.
Intergovernmental agreements that do not otherwise require city council approval as
provided in section 4.12.095 .
(Code 2009, § 4.12.020; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
The following words, terms and phrases, when used in this chapter, have the meanings
ascribed to them in this section, except where the context clearly indicates a different
meaning:
Architectural and engineering services means:
1.
Professional services of an architectural or engineering nature, as defined by state
law, which are required to be performed or approved by a person licensed, registered,
or certified to provide such services as described in this chapter;
2.
Professional services of an architectural or engineering nature performed by contract
that are associated with research, planning, development, design, construction, alteration,
or repair of real property; and
3.
Such other professional services of an architectural or engineering nature, or incidental
services, which members of the architectural and engineering professions (and individuals
in their employ) may logically or justifiably perform, including: studies, investigations,
surveying, mapping, tests, evaluations, consultations, comprehensive planning, program
management, conceptual designs, plans and specifications, value engineering, construction
phase services, soils engineering, drawing reviews, preparation of operating and maintenance
manuals, and other related services.
Best and final offer means the final proposal submitted after negotiations are complete that contains
the proposer's most favorable terms. May also be referred to as "BAFO."
Bid means a person's response to an invitation for bids.
Bidder (also offeror ) means a person responding to an invitation for bids.
Brand name or equal specifications means a specification limited to one or more items by manufacturers' names or catalogue
numbers to describe the standard of quality, performance and other salient characteristics
needed to meet the city requirements, and which provides for the submissions of equivalent
products.
Brand name specification means a specification limited to one or more items by manufacturers' names or catalogue
numbers.
Business means any corporation, partnership, individual, sole proprietorship, joint stock
company, joint venture or any other private legal entity.
Change order means a written order signed and issued by the designee of the funding department's
executive director or the purchasing and contracts manager, directing the contractor
to make changes to the contract.
Confidential information means any information which is available to an employee only because of the employee's
status as an employee of the city and is not a matter of public knowledge or available
to the public on request.
Construction means the process of building, altering, repairing, improving or demolishing any
public infrastructure facility, structure, building or other public improvement of
any kind to any real property. The term "construction" does not include the routine
operation, repair or maintenance of existing public infrastructure facilities, public
structures, buildings, or other public improvements to any real property.
Contract means all types of city agreements, regardless of what they may be called, for the
procurement or disposal of supplies, services or construction.
Contract amendment means any written alteration of specifications, delivery point, rate of delivery,
period of performance, price, quantity or other provision of any contract by agreement
of the parties to the contract.
Contractor means any person having a contract with the city.
Cost data means factual information concerning the cost of labor, material, overhead and other
cost elements which are expected to be incurred or which have been actually incurred
by the contractor in performing the contract.
Cost-reimbursement contract means a contract under which a contractor is reimbursed for costs which are allowable
and allocable in accordance with the contract terms and the provisions of this chapter,
and a fee, if any.
Data means recorded information, regardless of form or characteristic.
Design-bid-build means a project delivery method in which the city sequentially awards separate contracts,
the first award for architectural and engineering services to design the project and
the second award for construction of the project according to the design.
Design-build means a project delivery method in which the city awards a single contract for design
and construction of a project.
Designee means a duly authorized representative of a person holding a superior position.
Direct or indirect participation means involvement through decision, approval, disapproval, recommendation, preparation
of any part of a purchase request, influencing the content of any specification or
procurement standard, rendering of advice, investigation, auditing, or in any other
advisory capacity.
Electronic means electrical, digital, magnetic, optical, electromagnetic, or any other similar
technology.
Employee means an individual drawing a salary or wages from the city, whether elected or not,
any noncompensated individual performing personal services for the city or any department,
agency, commission, council, board or any other entity established by the executive
or legislative branch of the city and any noncompensated individual serving as an
elected official of the city.
Financial interest means:
1.
Partial or whole ownership of any property or business; or
2.
Holding a position in a business such as an officer, director, trustee, partner, or
the like, or holding any position of management.
Formal purchase threshold means the dollar amount above which the city generally must purchase goods or services
using sealed competitive purchase methods as provided in sections 4.12.110 and 4.12.120 . The amount is reviewed annually in the city financial policies.
Grant means the furnishing of assistance, whether financial or otherwise, to any person
or group to support a program authorized by law.
Gratuity means a payment, loan, subscription, advance, deposit of money, service or anything
of more than nominal value, present or promised, unless consideration of substantially
equal or greater value is received.
Immediate family means a spouse, domestic partner, children, parents, brothers, sisters, parents-in-law,
brothers-in-law, sisters-in-law, stepparent, stepbrother, stepsister and stepchild.
Infrastructure facility means a building; structure; or networks of buildings, structures, pipes, controls,
and equipment that provide transportation, utilities, or public safety services.
Invitation for bids (IFB) means all documents, whether attached or incorporated by reference, utilized for
soliciting sealed bids.
Local business means an active business that has:
1.
An unrevoked city sale and use tax license;
2.
A physical location with at least one employee operating legally within the city limits;
and
3.
Is not delinquent in any payment or filing obligation related to city taxes.
May denotes the permissive.
Micro purchase threshold means the dollar amount at or below which the city may purchase goods or services
using micro purchase procedures as provided in section 4.12.140 (B). The amount is reviewed annually in the city financial policies.
Must (also shall, will ) denotes the imperative.
Non-federal entity for the purpose of this document, means the city when the city is a grant recipient.
Offeror means a respondent to any type of solicitation.
Preference means a computation for determining the awardee in a purchasing process. No actual
change is made to the person's response.
Pricing data means factual information concerning prices for items substantially similar to those
being procured. Prices in this definition refer to offered or proposed selling prices,
historical selling prices and current selling prices. The definition of the term "pricing
data" refers to data relevant to both prime and subcontract prices.
Procurement means the buying, purchasing, renting, leasing or otherwise acquiring of any supplies,
services or construction. The term "procurement" also includes all functions that
pertain to the obtaining of any supply, service or construction, including description
of requirements, selection and solicitation of sources, preparation and award of contract,
and all phases of contract administration.
Professional services mean services rendered by members of a recognized profession or possessing a special
skill. Such services are generally acquired to obtain information, advice, training
or direct assistance.
Proposal means a person's response to a request for proposals.
Proposer (also offeror ) means a respondent to a request for proposals.
Public agency means a public entity subject to or created by the city.
Public notice means the distribution or dissemination of information to interested parties and/or
the general public using methods that are reasonably available. Such methods may include
publication in newspapers of general circulation, magazines, circulars, electronic
or paper mailing lists, and websites designated by the city and maintained for that
purpose.
Purchasing and contracts manager is the principal public purchasing official for the city except that when a designation
of purchasing authority has been made to a different city official pursuant to section 4.12.080 , purchasing and contracts manager, when used in this chapter and in reference to
specific purchasing activities, means that designated official. In all general references
to the purchasing and contracts manager's duties and responsibilities, the term "purchasing
and contracts manager" means the person occupying the position of purchasing and contracts
manager as provided in sections 4.12.060 and 4.12.070 .
Request for proposals (RFP) means all documents, whether attached or incorporated by reference, utilized for
soliciting proposals.
Request for quotation (RFQ) means all documents, whether attached or incorporated by reference, utilized for
soliciting quotations. RFQs are generally informal in nature but with written specifications.
Request for statement of qualifications (SOQ) means all documents, whether attached or incorporated by reference, utilized to obtain
statements of the qualifications of potential firms or consultants, prior to issuing
a solicitation.
Responsible bidder (also offeror, proposer ) means a person capable in all respects of fully performing the contract requirements
and possessing the integrity and reliability to ensure good faith performance.
Responsive bidder (also offeror, proposer ) means a person who has submitted a bid or proposal which conforms in all material
respects to the requirements set forth in the solicitation.
Scope of work means a detailed, written description of the actual or conceptual requirements of
the work or project to establish a clear understanding of what is required by the
city under the contract.
Services means the furnishing of labor, time or effort by a contractor, which may involve
to a lesser degree the delivery or supply of products. The term "services" does not
include employment agreements or collective bargaining agreements.
Signature means a manual or electronic identifier, or the electronic result of an authentication
technique attached to or logically associated with a record that is intended by the
person using it to have the same force and effect as a manual signature. Electronic
signatures are as allowed by the Uniform Electronic Transactions Act, C.R.S. title
24, article 71.3.
Small business means a local business with 35 or fewer employees.
Small purchase threshold means the dollar amount below which the city may purchase goods or services using
small purchase procedures as provided in section 4.12.140 (C). The amount is reviewed annually in the city financial policies.
Solicitation means any competitive procurement process.
Specification means any description of the physical or functional characteristics, or of the nature
of a supply, service or construction item. The term "specification" may include a
description of any requirement for inspection, testing, or preparing a supply, service
or construction item for delivery.
Supplies means all property including, but not limited to, equipment, materials, printing,
insurance and leases of real property, excluding land or a permanent interest in land.
Surplus stock means all supplies that are no longer needed or being used by the city.
Written or in writing means the product of any method of forming characters on paper, other materials,
or viewable screens, which can be read, retrieved, and reproduced, including information
that is electronically transmitted and stored.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.030; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2015-26, §
2, 5-19-2015; Ord. No. O-2016-59, § 2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
Procurement documents are public records to the extent provided in C.R.S. title 24,
article 72, as amended, and are available to the public as provided in such statute.
However, any party submitting a bid or proposal to the city may designate certain
portions confidential, proprietary or trade secret information, indicating the basis
for such designation. In the event a request is submitted for disclosure of such a
document, the city will forthwith notify the party submitting the bid or proposal
who will, within two business days, advise the city whether it objects to the disclosure
of the confidential, proprietary or trade secret information. The city will comply
with applicable law relating to public open records.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.040; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
The use of electronic media, including acceptance of electronic signatures, is authorized
consistent with the city's applicable regulatory or other guidance for use of such
media, so long as such guidance provides for:
A.
Appropriate security to prevent unauthorized access to the bidding, approval, and
award processes; and
B.
Accurate retrieval or conversion of electronic forms of such information into a medium
which permits inspection and copying.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.050; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011)
A.
Establishment. The position of the purchasing and contracts manager is established.
B.
Tenure. The purchasing and contracts manager is subject to, and will have all the duties,
rights and benefits applicable to regular employees of the city, as set forth in the
personnel rules of the city.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.060; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Principal purchasing and contracting official. Except as otherwise provided in this chapter, although pursuant to the Charter, the
city manager is a purchasing agent for the city, the city manager may authorize the
purchasing and contracts manager to serve as the principal purchasing and contracting
official for the city, and will be responsible for the procurement of materials, supplies,
equipment, services and construction in accordance with this chapter, as well as the
management and disposal of supplies.
B.
Duties. In accordance with this chapter, and subject to the supervision of the city manager
or designee, the purchasing and contracts manager will, in accordance with applicable
regulations:
1.
Procure or supervise the procurement of all supplies, services and construction needed
by the city;
2.
Dispose of surplus stock. Where transfer of surplus stock to other city departments
is not viable, the purchasing and contracts manager has the authority to sell, trade,
donate, or otherwise dispose of surplus stock wholly owned by the city. In the event
items were obtained using federal, state, or other grant funds or reimbursed by those
monies, and are now deemed surplus, the purchasing and contracts manager will take
into consideration methods for the highest possible potential return on federal, state,
or other grant funded equipment. The following steps will be taken in the disposition
of equipment purchased with federal assistance:
a.
The non-federal entity must request disposition instructions from the federal awarding
agency if required by the terms and conditions of the federal award. Disposition of
the equipment will be made as follows, in accordance with federal awarding agency
disposition instructions:
i.
Items of equipment with a current per unit fair market value of $5,000.00 or less
may be retained, sold, or otherwise disposed of with no further responsibility to
the federal awarding agency.
ii.
Except as provided in 2 C.F.R. § 200.312(b), or if the federal awarding agency fails
to provide requested disposition instructions within 120 days, items of equipment
with a current per-unit fair market value in excess of $5,000.00 may be retained by
the non-federal entity or sold. The federal awarding agency is entitled to an amount
calculated by multiplying the current market value or proceeds from sale by the federal
awarding agency's percentage of participation in the cost of the original purchase.
If the equipment is sold, the federal awarding agency may permit the non-federal entity
to deduct and retain from the federal share $500.00 or ten percent of the proceeds,
whichever is less, for its selling and handling expenses.
iii.
The non-federal entity may transfer title to the property to the federal government
or to an eligible third party provided that, in such cases, the non-federal entity
must be entitled to compensation for its attributable percentage of the current fair
market value of the property.
b.
In cases where a non-federal entity fails to take appropriate disposition actions,
the federal awarding agency may direct the non-federal entity to take disposition
actions.
3.
Establish and maintain programs for specifications development, contract administration,
inspection and acceptance of supplies, service and construction.
4.
For procurements funded by federal, state or other grants, the purchasing and contracts
manager, or designee, will review every procurement to ensure the avoidance of unnecessary
or duplicative items. Additionally, consideration will be given to consolidating or
breaking out procurements to obtain a more economical purchase, and where appropriate,
an analysis will be made of lease versus purchase alternatives and any other appropriate
analysis to determine the most economical approach.
C.
Operational procedures. Consistent with this chapter, the purchasing and contracts manager may establish
operational procedures and policies relating to the execution of his or her duties.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.070; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Notwithstanding the provisions of section 4.12.070 , the purchasing and contracts manager may, with the concurrence of the assistant
city manager for shared services, delegate procurement authority with respect to certain
supplies, services, or construction to other city officials when the purchasing and
contracts manager deems such delegation necessary for the effective procurement of
these supplies, services or construction. All such delegated procurements will be
conducted pursuant to this chapter and regulations promulgated hereunder. Pursuant
to this section, in the event the purchasing and contracts manager designates another
official to make procurement, all references to specific purchasing activities by
the purchasing and contracts manager in this chapter refers to and is applicable to
said designated purchasing official. The general duties of the purchasing and contracts
manager, as described in this chapter, are not applicable to designated purchasing
officials.
B.
Council approval is a condition precedent to any award of procurement contracts or
approval of change orders or contract modifications under the following circumstances:
1.
Any contract, change order, or contract modification for which sufficient funding
is not available and appropriated in the applicable fund;
2.
Any contract, change order, or contract modification for a capital improvement program
project for which sufficient funding is not available and appropriated;
3.
Any formal request generated by the city manager or by motion duly made and passed
by council that asks city council to approve any specific city contract as is deemed
necessary in order to protect the integrity and accountability of the procurement
system and/or to best serve the public interest;
4.
Any procurement contract that otherwise requires specific council action; or
5.
Exceptions. Where appropriated funds are available, such restrictions set forth in
this chapter are subject to waiver by the city manager when delay or job closure would
create an emergency and are found to be detrimental to the city's best interest. City
council will be notified of the action taken; however, it will not retroactively rescind
the emergency action taken by the city manager.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.080; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2014-02, §
2, 1-7-2014; Ord. No. O-2015-26, § 2, 5-19-2015; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Pursuant to section 3.3 of the Charter, the mayor may delegate to other city officials authority to sign,
or may allow the mayor's facsimile or electronic signature on, written contracts.
The mayor will issue a written list of such delegations and any terms or limitations,
to the purchasing and contracts manager. The purchasing and contracts manager will
file each such list and distribute it to affected officials.
B.
Unauthorized purchases; unlawful; void. Except as herein provided, it is unlawful
for any city officer or employee to order the purchase of any supplies or make any
contract other than as provided in this chapter, and any purchase ordered or contract
made contrary to the provisions of this chapter is void, and the city is not bound
thereby.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.090; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2015-26, §
2, 5-19-2015; Ord. No. O-2016-59, § 2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Except as provided in subsections (b) and (c) below, all intergovernmental agreements
between the city and other governmental entities shall be submitted to the city council
for review, and any approval thereof shall be by resolution of the city council.
B.
The mayor is authorized to execute agreements between the city and other governmental
entities when the proposed agreement is in the best interest of the city, does not
entail any significant policy considerations, and does not involve a direct monetary
payment by the city, or involves a direct monetary payment to be made by the city
in an amount that does not exceed a threshold set forth in a financial policy approved
by the city council.
C.
The mayor is authorized to execute amendments to agreements previously authorized
by the city council when the proposed amendment does not significantly alter the obligations
of the city under the agreement and the increase in monetary payment by the city,
if any, does not exceed ten percent of the original monetary payment required by the
city under the agreement to be amended.
D.
Pursuant to section 3.3 of the Charter, the mayor may delegate to other city officials authority to sign,
or may allow the mayor's facsimile or electronic signature on intergovernmental agreements
executed pursuant to this section 4.12.095 . Such delegation shall be made pursuant to the procedures of section 4.12.090 .
(Ord. No. O-2022-58 , § 2, 12-20-2022)
It is the policy of the city to procure supplies, services, and construction in a
manner that affords vendors a fair and equal opportunity to compete. Unless otherwise
authorized by law, the city awards all city contracts by one of the following methods:
A.
Competitive sealed bidding.
B.
Competitive sealed proposals.
C.
Professional services.
D.
Small purchases.
E.
Negotiated purchases.
F.
Emergency procurement.
G.
Cooperative purchasing agreements.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.100; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Conditions for use. The city awards all city contracts by competitive sealed bidding
solicitation except as otherwise provided in this chapter.
B.
The purchasing and contracts manager determines which of the methods of source selection,
as set forth in section 4.12.100 , will be used to procure goods and services in the best interest of the city.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.105; Ord. No. O-2015-26, § 2, 5-19-2015)
A.
Invitation for bids. An invitation for bids will be issued and include specifications and all contractual
terms and conditions applicable to the procurement.
B.
Public notice. The city will give adequate public notice of invitations for bids of not less than
14 calendar days prior to the date set forth therein for the opening of bids, unless
exigent circumstances require a shorter period, as determined by the purchasing and
contracts manager, who will document shortened timeframes and why a reduced schedule
is required. The purchasing and contracts manager will not shorten timeframes so as
to reduce competition. The public notice may include the use of an electronic solicitation
system. The public notice will state the place, date and time of bid opening.
C.
Bid opening. The city opens bids publicly in the presence of one or more witnesses at the time
and place designated in the invitation for bids. The city records the name of each
bidder, the amount of each bid and such other relevant information as the purchasing
and contracts manager deems appropriate. The record and each bid is open to public
inspection in accordance with section 4.12.040 .
D.
Late bids. The city will not open bids it receives after the due date and time and rejects such
bids as non-responsive.
E.
Bid acceptance and bid evaluation. Bids are unconditionally accepted without alteration or correction, except as authorized
in this chapter. Bids are evaluated based on the requirements set forth in the invitation
for bids, which may include criteria to determine acceptability such as inspection,
testing, quality, workmanship, delivery and suitability for a particular purpose.
Those criteria that will affect the bid price and be considered in evaluation for
award will be objectively measurable, such as discounts, transportation costs and
total or life-cycle costs. The invitation for bids will set forth the evaluation criteria
to be used. No criteria may be used in bid evaluations that are not set forth in the
invitation for bids.
F.
Withdrawals of responses and mistakes in responses. Upon written request, any bidder may withdraw its response prior to the specified
due date and time. The purchasing and contracts manager may allow a bidder to withdraw
its response after the specified due date and time but prior to award, provided:
1.
The bidder provides evidentiary proof that clearly and convincingly demonstrates that
the bidder made a mistake in the costs or other material matter provided;
2.
The mistake is clearly evident on the response; or
3.
The purchasing and contracts manager finds it is unconscionable not to allow the bidder
to withdraw its response.
When it appears to the city from a review of the response that a bidder has made a
mistake, the city will ask the bidder to confirm the bid. Situations in which the
city requests confirmation include apparent errors or a price unreasonably lower than
other submitted prices. Upon acknowledgment that the bidder erred, the bidder may
authorize the city to consider the response as submitted or the bidder may withdraw
its bid if the conditions set forth in this section are met.
The effect of minor informalities and insignificant mistakes on price, quantity, quality,
delivery, or contractual conditions is negligible. As such, minor informalities and
insignificant mistakes are matters of form rather than substance which the city can
waive or permit the bidder to correct without prejudice to other bidders. The purchasing
and contracts manager may waive such informalities or mistakes or allow the bidder
to correct them depending on which is in the best interest of the city.
The purchasing and contracts manager will document in writing any decision to permit
or deny correction or withdrawal of a bid under this section.
G.
Award. Contracts are awarded with reasonable promptness by appropriate written notice to
the lowest responsible and responsive bidder whose bid meets the requirements and
criteria set forth in the invitation for bids. In the event the low responsive and
responsible bid exceeds available funds as certified by the city manager or designee
the purchasing and contracts manager is authorized, when time or economic considerations
preclude resolicitation of work of a reduced scope, to negotiate an adjustment of
the bid price with the low responsive and responsible bidder, including changes in
bid requirements, in order to bring the bid within the amount of available funds if
changing bid requirements would not result in another bidder being the lowest responsible
and responsive bidder.
H.
Multi-step sealed bidding. A multi-step sealed invitation for bids is a two-phase process. The technical first
phase is composed of one or more steps in which bidders submit unpriced technical
offers to be evaluated by the city. The second phase considers only those bidders
whose technical offers were determined to be acceptable during the first phase. At
this time, their price bids will be opened and considered. Award is made to the lowest
priced, responsive and responsible bidder.
I.
Prequalification. When the purchasing and contracts manager deems it beneficial to prequalify potential
bidders, the city will prepare and advertise a statement of qualifications package
requesting statements of qualifications. The city will give public notice, as defined
in subsection B of this section, not less than seven calendar days prior to the date
established for receipt of statements of qualifications. The city will select qualified
bidders to submit full bids based on the evaluation of the ability to perform the
proposed work. No award may be made from a request for statement of qualifications.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.110; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2015-26, §
2, 5-19-2015; Ord. No. O-2016-59, § 2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Request for proposals. Proposals are solicited through a request for proposals (RFP) process.
B.
Public notice. The city will give adequate public notice of request for proposals in the same manner
as provided in section 4.12.110.B. The minimum time is 14 calendar days, unless exigent
circumstances require a shorter period, as determined by the purchasing and contracts
manager, who will document shortened timeframes and why a reduced schedule is required.
The purchasing and contracts manager will not shorten timeframes so as to reduce competition.
C.
Receipt of proposals. The purchasing and contracts manager will maintain a register of proposals received
containing the name of each proposer. The register is open for public inspection only
after the award or cancellation of the request for proposals. The city will not permit
disclosure of the contents of any proposal during the process of evaluation and negotiation.
D.
Late proposals. The city will not open proposals it receives after the due date and time and will
reject such proposals as non-responsive.
E.
Evaluation. The request for proposals will state the factors the city will use to evaluate proposals.
Except in the case of a no-cost contract, the evaluation criteria will include cost.
A committee of no less than three individuals evaluates all responsive proposals.
F.
Prequalification. When the purchasing and contracts manager deems it beneficial to prequalify potential
proposers, the city will prepare and advertise a statement of qualifications package,
requesting statements of qualifications. The city will give public notice, as defined
in section 4.12.110.B, not less than seven calendar days prior to the date established
for receipt of statements of qualifications. The city will select proposers to submit
full proposals based on the evaluation of a proposer's ability to perform the proposed
work. No award may be made from a request for statement of qualifications.
G.
Discussion with responsible proposers, clarification and negotiations. The city may discuss a responsive, responsible proposal with the proposer for the
purpose of clarifying the proposal to ensure full understanding of, and conformance
to, the solicitation requirements. The city accords all responsive, responsible proposers
fair and equal treatment with respect to clarification and scoring of proposals. The
city may permit negotiations with proposers after submissions, and prior to award,
for the purpose of obtaining best and final offers. In conducting discussions or negotiations,
the city will not disclose the identity of competing proposers or any information
derived from proposals submitted by competing proposers.
H.
Award. The city awards contracts to the responsive, responsible proposer whose proposal
it determines in writing is the most advantageous to the city, taking into consideration
the evaluation factors set forth in the applicable request for proposals. The city
will use no other factors or criteria in the evaluation. The purchasing and contracts
manager will ensure that the city retains the written determination upon which the
city made the award.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.120; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2015-26, §
2, 5-19-2015; Ord. No. O-2016-59, § 2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Authority. The city may procure the services of licensed professionals or consultants in accordance
with this section unless prohibited by the Charter. It may directly procure such services
not exceeding the small purchase threshold, as defined by the city's financial policies,
according to section 4.12.140 . In accordance with the Charter, no contract for the service of legal counsel may
be awarded without the approval of the city attorney.
B.
Selection procedure.
1.
Conditions for use. Except as provided under sections 4.12.140 , 4.12.150 through 4.12.160 , the city procures professional services in accordance with this section.
2.
Public announcement, statement of qualifications and request for proposals. The city may invite parties engaged in providing the required services to submit
a statement of qualifications through a public announcement. The request for statements
of qualifications will:
a.
Briefly describe the services the city requires; and
b.
List the types of information and date required from each offeror.
The city will review the statements of qualifications received and relevant performance
data to determine persons with sufficient qualifications to perform the work and invite
them to respond to a request for proposals. No award may be made from a request for
statement of qualifications. Requests for proposals will be in accordance with section 4.12.120 .
3.
Discussions. When procuring required professional services, the purchasing and contracts manager
may engage in discussions with any proposer that has submitted a proposal to determine
such proposer's qualifications for further consideration. In conducting discussions,
the city will not disclose the identity of competing proposers or any information
derived from proposals submitted by competing proposers.
4.
Award. The city awards contracts to the proposers it determines are best qualified based
on:
a.
The evaluation factors set forth in the request for proposals; and
b.
Negotiation of compensation determined to be fair and reasonable.
If compensation cannot be agreed upon with the best qualified proposer, the city will
terminate negotiations with the selected proposer. If proposals were submitted by
other proposers the city determines are qualified, the city may negotiate with such
other proposer or proposers, in the order of their respective qualification ranking
and the city may award a contract to the proposer ranked best qualified if the city
considers the amount of compensation fair and reasonable.
5.
Negotiated professional services contracts. Negotiated professional service contracts may be awarded without the benefit of formal
or informal bidding when the purchasing and contracts manager approves the negotiation
prior to award. Negotiated contracts will be based on professional services contracts
for specialized design, management, consulting or similar requirement which traditionally
are unique in their nature and not susceptible to formal bidding.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.130; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2015-26, §
2, 5-19-2015; Ord. No. O-2016-59, § 2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
General. The city may make purchases not exceeding the dollar amount indicated in the city's
financial policies in accordance with the micro or small purchase procedures authorized
in this section. The dollar amount of a purchase is determined by the total amount
of the transaction regardless of the number of items, funding source, delivery location,
et cetera. Purchases will not be artificially divided so as to constitute a micro
or small purchase under this section.
B.
Micro purchases. City departments and divisions may purchase supplies, services, professional services,
or construction in the open market, provided that no such purchase exceeds the dollar
amount allowed for micro purchases in the city's financial policies. The solicitation
of three quotations is encouraged but not required.
C.
Small purchases. When making purchases that exceed the dollar amount for micro purchases but that
do not exceed the dollar amount for small purchases in the city's financial policies,
the city will obtain quotes from an adequate number of offerors in the discretion
of the purchasing and contracts manager or designee. Award will be made to the offeror
providing the most advantageous quotation, with price being at least one consideration.
The city records and maintains as a public record in the office of purchasing and
contracts:
1.
The names of offerors submitting quotations;
2.
The date and amount of each quotation; and
3.
The reasons supporting the award where the award is to other than the lowest price.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.140; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2015-26, §
2, 5-19-2015; Ord. No. O-2016-59, § 2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
Purchase orders and contracts in any amount may be awarded without benefit of formal
or informal bidding in one or more of the following cases when, prior to award, the
purchasing and contracts manager approves use of negotiation:
A.
City share contracts. City share contracts which include contracts with developers for the construction
of public improvements whereby the city shares in the cost of construction as may
be required by the code or as the result of an annexation agreement or a similar agreement
in kind and/or a determination is made that it is in the best interest of the city
to share in the cost. The city reserves the right to be present at the bid openings;
to inspect all bids received by the developer; and, for good cause, to reject such
bids Additionally, the city reserves the right to perform or contract for the work
to be accomplished;
B.
Extension of existing contract. A vendor offers to extend an existing contract under the same conditions and at the
same or lower price, and such extension is in the best interest of the city. As used
in this section, the term "price" may be deemed "the same or lower" if the price from
the previous contract will be increased in the extension only in an amount equal to
an inflationary factor as determined by acceptable indices or criteria;
C.
Used, discontinued, unique purchases. Used, discontinued or otherwise unique equipment or commodities which become available
or are offered only for a limited time which the city can acquire below the cost of
similar new or regular items;
D.
Service on existing equipment. It is to the city's advantage to obtain parts, repair or service on existing equipment,
supplies, or software from a factory-authorized dealer or distributor. Where there
are two or more viable dealers or distributors, the city will seek competition pursuant
to sections 4.12.110 , 4.12.120 , or 4.12.130 ;
E.
Perishable supplies. Supplies that are perishable or cannot be purchased by ordinary procedures because
of imminent spoilage or decay;
F.
Reduced total cost. Closer location, more advantageous time allowances or similar variable factors can
reduce the total cost of the product or service;
G.
Standardization. Standardization of parts, modules, accessories or additions will minimize excessive
inventory or maintain compatibility with existing furnishings or installations;
H.
Lack of specifications. It is not feasible to prepare definite, certain and precise written specifications;
I.
No response. Competitive solicitation procedures have failed to provide sufficient responsive,
responsible bidders;
J.
Sole source. After a good-faith review of available sources, only one specific and responsible
source is known to exist for the required supplies or services and the purchasing
and contracts manager concurs in writing. A justification form for each sole source
request will be reviewed, approved and maintained for the city by the purchasing and
contracts manager. The purchasing and contracts manager will maintain a list of standard
sole source exceptions for certain routine purchases where bidding is impractical.
This list will be reviewed and updated no less than every two years;
K.
Resale. Where the city purchases a vendor's consumables for the purpose of resale;
L.
No cost contracts. Where the city will not expend public funds in the performance of the contract, the
purchasing and contracts manager will determine if such a contract is awarded by competitive
solicitation in the best interest of the city.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.150; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
Notwithstanding any provisions of this chapter, the purchasing and contracts manager
may make or authorize others to make emergency procurements of supplies, services
or construction items when there exists a threat to public health, welfare or safety,
provided that such emergency procurements will be made with such competition as is
practicable under the circumstances. A written determination of the basis for the
emergency and for the selection of the particular contractor will be included in the
contract file. As soon as practicable, a record of each emergency procurement will
be made and set forth the contractor's name, the amount and type of the contract,
a listing of the item procured under the contract, and the identification number of
the contract file.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.160; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
Notwithstanding any provisions of this chapter, in order to remediate emergencies
or disasters declared by the city manager under the authority of this Code, the purchasing
and contracts manager may make or authorize others to make emergency procurements
of supplies, services or construction items and may raise the contract amounts specified
in section 4.12.140 to align with current federal or state procurement guidelines, provided that such
emergency or disaster procurements are made with such competition as is practicable
under the circumstances.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.165; Ord. No. O-2013-50, § 1, 10-8-2013; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
The city may cancel invitations for bids, requests for proposals, or other solicitations
and may reject any bid or proposal in whole or in part when it serves the best interest
of the city. The city will record the reasons for such cancellation or rejection in
the procurement file. Each solicitation issued by the city will state that the solicitation
may be cancelled and that any bid or proposal may be rejected in whole or in part
when rejection or cancellation is in the best interest of the city. The city will
publish a notice of cancellation on the electronic solicitation system, or otherwise
make available to all businesses solicited. The notice will identify the solicitation
and explain the reason for cancellation or rejection.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.170; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Determination of nonresponsibility. If the city determines an offeror to whom it would otherwise award a contract is
nonresponsible, the purchasing and contracts manager will make a written determination
of nonresponsibility, setting forth the basis of such finding. The unreasonable failure
of an offeror to promptly supply information in connection with an inquiry with respect
to responsibility may be grounds for a determination of nonresponsibility with respect
to such offeror. A copy of the determination will be sent promptly to the nonresponsible
offeror. The final determination will be made part of the contract file.
B.
Right of nondisclosure. Upon request of an offeror, the city will not publicly disclose nonpublic information
furnished pursuant to this section.
C.
Prequalification of supplier and contractors. The city may prequalify prospective suppliers for particular types of supplies, services
and construction. The purchasing and contracts manager determines the method of submitting
prequalification information and the information required in order to be prequalified.
Prequalification is not a conclusive determination of responsibility.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.180; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
Whenever the city anticipates seeking or receiving federal funds as reimbursement
for, or other form of payment or contribution toward a city contract or project, the
city and any party contracting with the city for such work must take all necessary
affirmative steps to assure that minority businesses, women's business enterprises,
and labor surplus area firms are used when possible. Affirmative steps must include:
A.
Placing qualified small and minority businesses and women's business enterprises on
solicitation lists;
B.
Assuring that small and minority businesses, and women's business enterprises are
solicited whenever they are potential sources;
C.
Dividing total requirements, when economically feasible, into smaller tasks or quantities
to permit maximum participation by small and minority business, and women's business
enterprises;
D.
Establishing delivery schedules, where the requirement permits, which encourage participation
by small and minority business, and women's business enterprises;
E.
Using the services and assistance, as appropriate, of such organizations as the small
business administration, and the minority business development agency of the department
of commerce; and
F.
Requiring the prime contractors, if subcontracts are to be let, to take the affirmative
steps listed in subsections A through E of this section.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.185; Ord. No. O-2015-26, § 2, 5-19-2015; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
For contracts awarded pursuant to sections 4.12.110 and 4.12.140.C:
1.
Local businesses, as defined in section 4.12.030 , will be provided a five percent preference for sums not exceeding $100,000.00. For
sums exceeding $100,000.00, a five percent preference will be applied to first $100,000.00
of the bid.
2.
Small businesses as defined in section 4.12.030 , which are first determined to be local, will be provided a 2.5 percent preference
in addition to the local business preference for sums not exceeding $100,000.00. For
sums exceeding $100,000.00, an additional 2.5 percent preference will be applied to
first $100,000.00 of the bid.
B.
For contracts awarded pursuant to section 4.12.120 :
1.
Local businesses, as defined in section 4.12.030 , will be provided a five percent preference in the scoring of proposals.
2.
Small businesses, as defined in section 4.12.030 , which are first determined to be local, will be provided a 2.5 percent preference
in addition to the local business preference.
C.
The following exceptions apply:
1.
Purchases made in accordance with external funding source requirements that preclude
application of local and/or small business preferences.
2.
Purchases made in accordance with sections 4.12.160 or 4.12.165 .
(Code 2009, § 4.12.190; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Substantiation of offered prices. The city, at its discretion, may request factual information reasonably available
to the bidder prior to award of a contract to substantiate that the price or cost
offered, or some portion of it, is reasonable if the price is not:
1.
Based on adequate price competition;
2.
Based on established catalogue or market prices; or
3.
Set by law or regulation.
B.
Required submissions relating to change orders or contract modifications. The city may request that a contractor submit cost or pricing data pursuant to the
pricing of any change order or contract modification, including adjustments to contracts
awarded by competitive sealed bidding, whether or not cost or pricing data was required
in connection with initial pricing of the contract.
C.
Exceptions. The submission of cost or pricing data relating to the pricing of a change order
or contract modification is not required when:
1.
Unrelated and separately priced adjustments for which cost or pricing data would not
be required are consolidated for administrative convenience; or
2.
It is determined in writing by the purchasing and contracts manager that the requirements
of subsection B of this section may be waived, and the determination states the reasons
for such waiver.
D.
Certification required. A contractor, actual or prospective, required to submit cost or pricing data in accordance
with this section, must certify that, to the best of the contractor's knowledge and
belief, the cost or pricing data submitted was accurate, complete and current as of
a mutually specified date prior to the award of the contract or the pricing of the
change order or contract modification.
E.
Price adjustment provision required. Any contract award, change order or contract modification under which the submission
and certification of cost or pricing data are required must contain a provision stating
that the price to the city, including profit or fee, will be adjusted to exclude any
significant sums by which the city finds that such price was increased because the
contractor-furnished cost or pricing data was inaccurate, incomplete or not current
as of the date agreed upon between the city and the contractor.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.200; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
The purchasing and contracts manager may require bid and performance bonds or other
adequate security may be requested for supply contracts or service contracts as the
purchasing and contracts manager deems advisable to protect the city's interests.
The city will set forth any such bonding requirements in the solicitation. The city
will not use bid and performance bonds as a substitute for a determination of an offeror's
responsibility.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.210; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
General authority. Subject to the limitations of this section, any type of contract which is appropriate
to the procurement and which will promote the best interests of the city may be used,
provided that the use of a cost-plus-a-percentage-of-cost contract is prohibited.
A cost-reimbursement contract may be used only when a determination is made in writing
that such contract is likely to be less costly to the city than any other type or
that it is impracticable to obtain the supply, service or construction items required
except under such a contract, and the contract nevertheless establishes a maximum
reimbursable amount which may not be exceeded without written approval by the city.
B.
Approval of accounting system. The city will require contractors to submit appropriate documentation prior to the
award of cost-reimbursement contracts confirming that the proposed contractor's accounting
system:
1.
Will permit timely development of all necessary cost data in the form required by
the specific contract type contemplated; and
2.
Is adequate to allocate costs in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles.
C.
Multi-term contracts.
1.
Use. Unless otherwise provided by law, the city may enter into a contract for supplies
or services for any period of time deemed to be in the best interests of the city,
provided:
a.
The term of the contract and conditions of renewal or extension, if any, are included
in the solicitation;
b.
Funds are available for the first fiscal period of the contracting period;
c.
Payment and performance obligations for succeeding fiscal periods will be subject
to the availability and appropriation of funds;
d.
Estimated requirements cover the period of the contract and are reasonably firm and
continuing; and
e.
Such a contract will serve the best interest of the city by encouraging effective
competition or otherwise promoting economies in the city procurement.
Contract terms in excess of five years require the approval of the purchasing and
contracts manager.
2.
Cancellation due to unavailability of funds in succeeding fiscal periods. When funds are not appropriated or otherwise made available to support continuation
of performance in a subsequent fiscal period, the city will cancel the contract and
reimburse the contractor for nonrecurring costs incurred but not amortized in the
price of the supplies or services being delivered under the contract, all to the extent
authorized by law.
D.
Multiple-source contracting.
1.
General. A multiple-source award is an award of an indefinite quantity contract for one or
more similar supplies or services to more than one offeror. The obligation to order
the city's actual requirements is limited by the provision of C.R.S. § 4-2-306(1)
as amended.
2.
Limitations on use. A multiple-source award may be made when awards to two or more offerors for similar
products are necessary for adequate delivery, service or product compatibility. Any
multiple-source award will be made in accordance with provisions of sections 4.12.110 , 4.12.120 , 4.12.130 , 4.12.140 , 4.12.160 and 4.12.165 as applicable. Awards will not be made for the purpose of dividing the business,
making available product or supplier selection to allow for user preference unrelated
to utility or economy, or avoiding the resolution of tie bids. Any such awards will
be limited to the least number of suppliers necessary to meet the valid requirements.
3.
Contract and solicitation provisions. The city shall name all eligible users of the contract in the solicitation except
that the city may add additional users to the contract upon approval of the contractor,
where the needs are as described in the solicitation and doing so is in the best interests
of the city. The city shall obtain the actual requirements of such users in accordance
with the contract provided that:
a.
The city reserves the right to take bids separately if a particular quantity requirement
arises which exceeds its normal requirement or an amount specified in the contract;
and
b.
The city reserves the right to take bids separately if the purchasing and contracts
manager approves a finding that the supply or service available under the contract
will not meet a nonrecurring special need of the city.
4.
Intent to use. If a multiple-source award is anticipated prior to issuing a solicitation, the city
reserves the right to make such an award and the criteria for award will be stated
in the solicitation.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.220; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Contract clauses. All city contracts for supplies, services and construction will include provisions
necessary to define the responsibilities and rights of the parties to the contract.
The purchasing and contracts manager, after consultation with the city attorney, may
issue clauses appropriate for supply, service or construction contracts, addressing,
among others, the following subjects:
1.
The unilateral right of the city to order in writing changes in the work within the
scope of the contract;
2.
The unilateral right of the city to order in writing temporary stopping of the work
or delaying performance;
3.
