Variations occurring between estimated quantities of work in contract and actual quantities;
4.
Defective pricing;
5.
Liquidated damages;
6.
Specified excuses for delay or nonperformance;
7.
Termination of the contract for default;
8.
Termination of the contract in whole or in part for the convenience of the city;
9.
Suspension of work on a construction project ordered by the city;
10.
Site conditions differing from those indicated in the contract or ordinarily encountered,
except that a differing site conditions clause need not be included in a contract:
a.
When the contract is negotiated;
b.
When the contractor provides the site or design; or
c.
When the parties have otherwise agreed with respect to the risk of differing site
conditions.
B.
Price adjustments.
1.
Adjustments in price resulting from the use of contract clauses required by subsection
A of this section will be computed in one or more of the following ways:
a.
By agreement on a fixed price adjustment before commencement of the pertinent performance
or as soon thereafter as practicable;
b.
By unit prices specified in the contract or subsequently agreed upon;
c.
By the costs attributable to the events or situations under such clauses with adjustment
of profit or fee, all as specified in the contract or subsequently agreed upon;
d.
In such other manner as the contracting parties may mutually agree; or
e.
In the absence of agreement by the parties, by a unilateral determination by the city
of the costs attributable to the events or situations under such clauses with adjustment
of profit or fee as computed by the city, as accounted for in accordance with applicable
cost principles and subject to the provisions of section 4.12.400 through and including section 4.12.430 .
2.
A contractor is required to submit cost or pricing data if any adjustment in contract
price is subject to the provisions of section 4.12.200 .
C.
Standard clauses and their modification. The purchasing and contracts manager, after consultation with the city attorney,
may establish standard contract clauses for use in the city contracts. If the purchasing
and contracts manager establishes any standard clauses addressing the subjects set
forth in subsection A of this section, such clauses may be varied at the request of
the funding department's executive director, or designee, and upon approval of the
purchasing and contracts manager, and provided that notice of any such material variation
be stated in the invitation for bids or request for proposals.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.230; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A contract administration system designed to ensure that a contractor is performing
in accordance with the solicitation under which the contract was awarded, and the
terms and conditions of the contract, will be maintained.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.240; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
The city may, at reasonable times, inspect the part of the plant, place of business
or worksite of a contractor or subcontractor at any tier which is pertinent to the
performance of any contract awarded or to be awarded by the city.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.250; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011)
A.
Audit or cost of pricing data. The city may, at reasonable times and places, audit the books and records of any
contractor who has submitted cost or pricing data pursuant to section 4.12.200 to the extent that such books, documents, papers and records are pertinent to such
cost or pricing data. Any person who receives a contract, change order or contract
modification for which cost or pricing data is required, shall maintain such books,
documents, papers and records that are pertinent to such cost or pricing data for
three years from the date of final payment under the contract.
B.
Contract audit. The city is entitled to audit the books and records of a contractor or a subcontractor
at any tier under any negotiated contract or subcontract other than a firm fixed-price
contract to the extent that such books, documents, papers and records are pertinent
to the performance of such contract or subcontract. Such books and records must be
maintained by the contractor for a period of three years from the date of final payment
under the prime contract and by the subcontractor for a period of three years from
the date of final payment under the subcontract.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.260; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
When for any reason collusion or other anticompetitive practices are suspected among
any offerors, a notice of the relevant facts will be transmitted to the city attorney,
the state attorney general and the Boulder County District Attorney.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.270; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Contract file. All determinations and other written records pertaining to the solicitation, award
or performance of a contract will be maintained for the city by the purchasing and
contracts manager.
B.
Retention of procurement records. All procurement records will be retained and disposed of by the city in accordance
with the applicable records retention guidelines and schedules approved by the city
council.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.280; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
Decisions by city employees authorized by this chapter are final.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.290; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011)
The purchasing and contracts manager has the authority to approve purchases from cooperative
purchasing agreements when the best interests of the city would be served thereby.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.295; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
All specifications will be drafted so as to promote overall economy for the purposes
intended and encourage competition in satisfying the city's needs, and will not be
unduly restrictive. The policy enunciated in this section applies to all specifications,
including, but not limited to, those prepared for the city by architects, engineers,
designers and draftsmen.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.300; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
Specifications including references to brand name, brand name or equal, or qualified
product list may be used when the purchasing and contracts manager determines it to
be appropriate and not unduly restrictive.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.310; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011)
The purchasing and contracts manager has discretion to select the appropriate method
of construction-contracting management of a particular project. In determining which
method to use, the purchasing and contracts manager will consider the city's requirements,
its resources and the potential contractor's capabilities. Except where the city uses
a design-bid-build approach, the purchasing and contracts manager shall include in
the contract file a written statement setting forth the reason for selection of a
particular method of construction-contracting management for each project.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.320; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Unless otherwise provided in this chapter, the city awards all city contracts for
infrastructure facilities and services by competitive solicitation.
B.
The purchasing and contracts manager will determine which of the methods of source
selection, as set forth in section 4.12.100 , will be used to procure goods and services in the best interest of the city.
C.
When the source selection method for infrastructure facilities and services is a request
for proposals, in addition to requirements set forth in section 4.12.120 , each request for proposal:
1.
Should include design or performance requirements;
2.
May allow payment of stipends to the unsuccessful proposers when the city determines
that the cost of preparing proposals is high in view of the size, estimated price,
and complexity of the procurement. The amount of such stipend and the terms under
which stipends will be paid must be stated in the request for proposals.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.330; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2015-26, §
2, 5-19-2015; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Requirement for bid security. The city will require bid security for all competitive solicitations for construction
contracts when the purchasing and contracts manager estimates the price will exceed
$100,000.00. Bid security must be a bond provided by a surety company authorized to
do business in the state, or the equivalent in cash, or otherwise supplied in a form
satisfactory to the city. Nothing herein prevents the requirement of such bonds on
construction contracts under $100,000.00. When the circumstances warrant, the city
may waive this bonding for all parties bidding.
B.
Rejection of bids for noncompliance with bid security requirements. When the solicitation requires security, noncompliance requires the city to reject
the bid unless the city determines that the bid fails to comply only in a nonsubstantial
manner with the security requirements.
C.
Withdrawal of bids. After bids are opened, they are irrevocable for the period specified in the invitation
for bids (except as provided for in section 4.12.110 ). If the city permits a bidder to withdraw its bid before award or excludes the bidder
from the competition before award, the city will take no action against the bidder
or the bid security.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.340; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2015-26, §
2, 5-19-2015; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
When required; amounts. Construction contracts in excess of $100,000.00 require that the following bonds
or security be delivered to the city and be binding on the parties upon the execution
of the contract:
1.
A performance bond satisfactory to the city, executed by a surety company authorized
to do business in the state or otherwise secured in a manner satisfactory to the city,
in an amount equal to 100 percent of the price specified in the contract;
2.
A payment bond satisfactory to the city, executed by a surety company authorized to
do business in the state or otherwise executed in a manner satisfactory to the city,
for the protection of all persons supplying labor and material to the contractor or
its subcontractors for the performance of the work provided for in the contract. The
bond will be in the amount equal to 100 percent of the price specified in the contract;
3.
The purchasing and contracts manager may require a performance bond for any contract
less than $100,000.00;
4.
When circumstances warrant, the purchasing and contracts manager, with the approval
of the city attorney, may approve other forms of acceptable securities.
B.
Reduction of bond amounts. With the concurrence of the funding department's executive director, or designee,
the purchasing and contracts manager is authorized to reduce the amount of the performance
and payment bonds to 50 percent of the contract price for each bond when the purchasing
and contracts manager makes a written determination it is in the best interests of
the city to do so.
C.
Authority to require additional bonds. Nothing in this section will be construed to limit the authority of the city to require
a performance bond or other security in addition to those bonds, or in circumstances
other than specified in subsection A of this section.
D.
Suits on payment bonds; right to institute. Unless otherwise authorized by law, any person who has furnished labor or material
to the contractor or subcontractors for the work provided in the contract, for which
a payment bond is furnished under this section, and who has not been paid in full
within 90 days from the date on which the person performed the last of the labor or
supplied the material, has the right to sue on the payment bond for any amount unpaid
at the time the suit is instituted and to prosecute the action for the amount due
that person. However, any party that contracts with a subcontractor of a city contractor
but does not have an express or implied contract with the city contractor will have
a right of action against the payment bond upon:
1.
Giving written notice to the city contractor within 90 days of the date on which that
party performed the last of the labor or supplied the last of the material upon which
such claim is made; and
2.
Stating with substantial accuracy:
a.
The amount claimed; and
b.
The name of the party to whom the material was supplied or for whom the labor was
performed; and
3.
Serving the notice personally or by other method of transmittal that confirms actual
delivery to the contractor at any place the contractor maintains an office or conducts
its business.
E.
Suits on payment bonds; where and when brought. Unless otherwise authorized by law, every suit instituted upon a payment bond must
be brought in a court of competent jurisdiction for the county or district in which
the construction contract was to be performed, but no such suit may be commenced after
the expiration of one year after the day on which the last of the labor was performed
or material was supplied by the person bringing suit. The obligee named in the bond
need not be joined as a party in any such suit.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.350; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
Any person may request and obtain from the city a certified copy of a bond upon payment
of the cost of reproduction of the bond and postage, if any. A certified copy of a
bond is prima facie evidence of the contents, execution and delivery of the original.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.360; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Authority.
1.
After reasonable notice to a person and reasonable opportunity for that person to
be heard by the purchasing and contracts manager, then the purchasing and contracts
manager, after consulting with the city manager, or designee, and the city attorney,
has the authority to suspend and/or debar that person for cause from consideration
for award of contracts.
2.
Suspension of a person may be issued for cause at the discretion of the purchasing
and contracts manager without further action for debarment of that person. Additionally,
pending hearing on debarment, the purchasing and contracts manager has the authority
to suspend a person from consideration for award of contracts if there is probable
cause for debarment. The suspension shall also extend to work as a subcontractor on
any city contract.
B.
Causes for debarment or suspension. The causes for debarment or suspension include:
1.
Conviction, within the last five years, of a criminal offense as an incident to obtaining
or attempting to obtain a public or private contract or subcontract, or in the performance
of such contract or subcontract;
2.
Conviction, within the last five years, under state or federal statutes of embezzlement,
theft, forgery, bribery, falsification or destruction of records, receiving stolen
property or any other offense indicating a lack of business integrity or business
honesty which currently, seriously and directly affects responsibility as a city contractor;
3.
Conviction, within the last five years, under state or federal antitrust statutes
arising out of the submission of bids or proposals;
4.
Violation of contract provisions within the last five years, as set forth below, of
a character which is regarded by the purchasing and contracts manager to be so serious
as to justify debarment action:
a.
Deliberate failure without good cause to perform in accordance with the specifications
or within the time limit provided in the contract; or
b.
Failure to perform or unsatisfactory performance in accordance with the terms of one
or more contracts, provided that failure to perform or unsatisfactory performance
caused by acts beyond the control of the contractor is not a basis for debarment.
5.
Any other cause the purchasing and contracts manager determines to be so serious and
compelling as to affect responsibility as a city contractor, including debarment by
another governmental entity for any cause listed in this chapter;
6.
Violation of the ethical standards set forth in section 4.12.450 through and including section 4.12.530 ;
7.
Default on the payment of taxes, licenses, or other monies lawfully due the city;
and
8.
Suspension or debarment by any other unit of government constitutes prima facie evidence
of grounds for debarment by the city during the period of such suspension or debarment
elsewhere. However, a party may petition for review pursuant to the appeal process
provided in this Code and the city will approve eligibility if the party demonstrates
that it has not violated the city's standard for debarment.
C.
Term of suspension or debarment.
1.
Suspension will not be for a period of more than one year. If, however, the suspension
is issued as a precedent to debarment proceedings, suspension will not be for a period
of more than three months while the debarment decision is pending. Debarment will
not be for a period of more than three years.
2.
After the term of the suspension or debarment has passed, the person has the right
to request reinstatement to good standing with the city. The person has the burden
of demonstrating and certifying to the purchasing and contracts manager that they
have remedied the cause that prompted the suspension and/or debarment. The purchasing
and contracts manager has the authority under section 4.12.390 to make a final decision whether to reinstate the person.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.370; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
The purchasing and contracts manager shall issue a written decision regarding debarment
or suspension, stating the reasons for such action, and shall mail or otherwise furnish
the decision directly to the debarred or suspended party and any party intervening.
B.
The purchasing and contracts manager shall issue a written decision regarding reinstatement,
stating the reasons and justification for such action, and shall mail or otherwise
furnish the decision directly to the debarred or suspended party and any party intervening.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.380; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016)
A.
A decision under section 4.12.380 is final and conclusive, unless the debarred or suspended person within ten days
after the mailing or service of the decision takes an appeal to the city council,
in writing, specifying all grounds of error alleged in the decision.
B.
Nothing contained in this appeal process is designed or intended to create or confer
any private right of action; rather, the appeal mechanism will serve to promote public
scrutiny and remedy through public hearing and decision by the elected body of the
city, before the public forum.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.390; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Right to protest. Any actual or prospective bidder or proposer aggrieved in connection with the solicitation
or award of a contract may submit a written protest to the purchasing and contracts
manager. Actual or prospective bidders must submit protests within three calendar
days of the date they learned of or reasonably should have learned of facts giving
rise to the protest. A protest based upon restrictive specifications or alleged improprieties
in any type of solicitation must be filed no later than three calendar days prior
to bid opening or closing date for proposals, unless the aggrieved party did not know
and should not have known of the facts giving rise to such protest prior to three
days before bid opening or three days before the closing date for proposals.
B.
Form of protest. Protest must be in writing and include the following:
1.
The name and address of the protestor;
2.
Bid number of the procurement;
3.
Reason for the protest;
4.
Any available exhibits, evidence or documents supporting the protest.
C.
Appeal to city manager. If in the event that written response by the purchasing and contracts manager sustains
the prior position of the city, the protestor may resubmit to the city manager, or
designee, as a prerequisite to consideration by the city council, within three days
after mailing of ruling by the purchasing and contracts manager, a statement ruling
as to the propriety of the purchasing and contracts manager's response.
D.
Appeal to city council. If in the event that written response by the city manager sustains the prior position
of the city, the protestor may resubmit to the city council, within three days after
mailing of the ruling by the city manager, a statement ruling as to the propriety
of the city manager's and purchasing and contracts manager's responses.
E.
Stay of procurements during protest. In the event of a timely protest or appeal under this section, the purchasing and
contracts manager shall not proceed further with the solicitation or award of the
contract until the protest is resolved, unless the city determines that:
1.
The items to be procured are urgently required;
2.
Delivery or performance will be unduly delayed by failure to make the award promptly;
or
3.
Failure to make the award promptly will otherwise cause undue harm to the city.
F.
Entitlement to costs. When a protest is sustained by the city and the protestor should have been awarded
the contract under the solicitation but, due to a defect in the solicitation was not,
the protestor is entitled to the reasonable costs it incurred in connection with responding
to the solicitation. No other costs will be permitted, and reasonable costs will not
include attorneys' fees.
G.
Delays. All protests which delay the award of or timely compliance with the contract being
the subject matter of the protest must be accompanied by financial security in the
form of cash or an irrevocable letter of credit in an amount established by the city
manager to reflect the losses which may be occasioned by such delay, due to change
in economic and practical circumstances; however, this would be in an amount not less
than 25 percent of the budgeted allocation for the goods or service. In the event
that the decision by the city ultimately sustains the position of the purchasing and
contracts manager, all acquisition and administrative costs incurred by the city resulting
from the protest and delay will be withheld as compensation for the city, and any
remaining sum held as security will be returned to the protesting party.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.400; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Applicability. This section applies to controversies between the city and a contractor and which
arise under, or by virtue of, a contract between them. This includes, without limitation,
controversies based upon breach of contract, mistake, misrepresentation, or other
cause for contract modifications or rescission.
B.
Authority. Prior to commencement of an action in a court concerning the controversy, the contractor
must submit all contract claims against the city in writing to the purchasing and
contracts manager. Each claim must clearly and briefly state the grounds, and must
summarize or include all of the contractor's supporting evidence. The contractor may
request a conference with the purchasing and contracts manager on the claim. The purchasing
and contracts manager shall consider the merits of the claim.
C.
Notice to the contractor of the purchasing and contracts manager's decision. After consultation with the city attorney, the purchasing and contracts manager shall
promptly mail to the last known address of, or otherwise furnish a written decision
to, the contractor. The decision must state the reasons supporting it.
D.
Finality of the purchasing and contracts manager's decision; contractor's right to
appeal. The purchasing and contracts manager's decision is final and conclusive unless, within
ten calendar days from the mailing or delivery of the decision, whichever comes first,
the contractor mails or otherwise delivers a written appeal to the city manager. Each
appeal must clearly and briefly state the grounds. Promptly after receipt of the appeal,
the city manager shall mail to the last known address of, or otherwise furnish a written
decision directly to the contractor. The decision must state the reasons supporting
it. The city manager's decision is final.
E.
Failure to render timely decisions. If the purchasing and contracts manager does not issue the written decision required
by this section within 120 days after submittal of a claim and all supporting evidence,
or such longer period as may be agreed upon by the parties, then the contractor may
proceed as if an adverse decision had been received.
F.
Access to administrative forums. In the event that the contractor submits an appeal to the city manager, or designee,
the city manager, or designee, shall review the claim, including the written appeal
of the contractor and the decision of the purchasing and contracts manager, and within
ten calendar days from the date of receipt of the appeal shall mail or otherwise deliver
a written decision to the contractor, which decision constitutes the final position
of the city with respect to the contract claim. The city manager's, or designee's,
decision is final and conclusive.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.410; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
The purchasing and contracts manager, after consultation with the city manager, the
city attorney and the designee of the funding department's executive director, is
authorized to settle any protest regarding the solicitation or award of a city contract,
or any claim arising out of the performance of a city contract, prior to an appeal
to the city manager, or designee. However, no settlement may pledge the credit of
the city or otherwise violate any applicable provisions of the Charter or ordinances.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.420; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
Prior to award. If, prior to the award of a contract, the purchasing and contracts manager, after
consultation with the city attorney, determines that a solicitation is in violation
of federal, state or municipal law, the purchasing and contracts manager will cancel
the solicitation or proposal or revise it to comply with applicable law.
B.
After award. If, after an award, the purchasing and contracts manager, after consultation with
the city attorney, determines that a solicitation or award of a contract was in violation
of applicable law, the purchasing and contracts manager may:
1.
If a contractor has not acted fraudulently or in bad faith:
a.
Deem the contract voidable and ratify and affirm it, provided any fraudulent or illegal
terms are removed and that ratifying and affirming the contract is in the best interest
of the city; or
b.
Terminate the contract and compensate the contractor for the actual expenses it reasonably
incurred under the contract prior to the termination, plus a reasonable profit.
2.
If a contractor has acted fraudulently or in bad faith:
a.
Declare the contract null and void; or
b.
Ratify and affirm the contract, provided any fraudulent or illegal terms are removed,
if doing so is in the best interests of the city, without prejudice to the city's
rights to such damages as may be appropriate.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.430; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
To the extent that violations of the ethical standards of conduct set forth in this
article constitute violations of the C.R.S., as amended, they will be punishable as
provided therein. Such penalties shall be in addition to the civil sanctions set forth
in this section. Criminal, civil and administrative sanctions against employees or
nonemployees which are in existence on the effective date of the ordinance from which
this chapter is derived will not be impaired.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.450; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
A.
No employee, nor any business owned in whole or in part by an employee may contract
with the city for supplies, services or construction.
B.
It is unlawful and unethical for any city employee or city official to participate
directly or indirectly in a procurement process when:
1.
The city employee or city official or any member of the employee's or official's immediate
family has a financial interest in the contract at issue; or
2.
Any other person or business with whom the city employee's or official's immediate
family is negotiating or has an arrangement concerning prospective employment is involved
in the procurement process.
C.
An employee or official or any member of an employee's or official's immediate family
who holds a financial interest in a disclosed independently managed fund or account
in which the employee-beneficiary has no management rights and in which the employee-beneficiary
is not given notice of alterations in, or other dispositions of, the property subject
to such fund or account shall not be deemed to have a conflict of interest with regard
to matters pertaining to that financial interest.
D.
Discovery of actual or potential conflict of interest. Upon discovery of an actual
or potential conflict of interest, employees and officials must:
1.
Promptly notify the purchasing and contracts manager; and
2.
Withdraw from further participation in the transaction involved.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.460; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
It is unlawful and unethical for a city employee or city official who is participating
directly or indirectly in a procurement decision to become or to be an employee of
any party attempting to contract.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.470; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011)
The city manager, or designee, upon due notice to the city council, may waive an employee
or official's conflict of interest in section 4.12.460 or the restriction against contemporaneous employment in section 4.12.470 upon a written determination that:
A.
The contemporaneous employment or financial interest of the employee or official has
been publicly disclosed;
B.
The employee or official will be able to perform their procurement process function
without actual or apparent bias or impropriety; and
C.
The award will be in the best interest of the city.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.480; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011)
A.
Gratuities. It is unlawful and unethical for any person to offer, give or agree to give any city
employee, city official or former city employee, or for any city employee, city official
or former city employee to solicit, demand, accept or agree to accept from another
person, a gratuity or an offer of employment in connection with any decision, approval,
disapproval, recommendation or preparation of any part of a program requirement or
a purchase request, influencing the content of any specification or procurement standard,
rendering of advice, investigation, auditing or in any other advisory capacity in
any proceeding or application, request for ruling, determination, claim or controversy,
or other particular matter, pertaining to any program requirement or a contract or
subcontract, or to any solicitation or proposal therefor.
B.
Kickbacks. It is unlawful and unethical for any payment, gratuity or offer of employment to
be made by or on behalf of a subcontractor under a contract to the prime contractor
or higher tier subcontractor of any person associated therewith, as an inducement
for the award of a subcontract or order.
C.
Contract clause. The prohibition against gratuities and kickbacks prescribed in this section must
be conspicuously set forth in every contract and solicitation therefor.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.490; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
It is unlawful and unethical for a person to be retained, or to retain a person, to
solicit or secure a city contract upon an agreement or understanding for a commission,
percentage, brokerage or contingent fee, except for retention of bona fide employees
or bona fide established commercial selling agencies for the purpose of securing business.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.500; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011)
It is unlawful and unethical for a current or former employee to knowingly use confidential
information for actual or anticipated personal gain, or for the actual or anticipated
personal gain of any other person.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.510; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011)
The city manager, or designee, may impose any remedy outlined by the personnel rules
of the city relating to discipline of city employees.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.520; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011)
The city is entitled to recover the value of anything transferred or received in breach
of the ethical standards of this chapter from any person by bringing an appropriate
action before any court of competent jurisdiction.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.530; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
In order to provide necessary land, buildings, equipment and other property for governmental
or proprietary purposes, the city is authorized to enter into long-term or short-term
rental or leasehold agreements, but in no event will this be construed as authorizing
the use by the city of leasehold agreements to finance residential housing. Such agreements
may include an option to purchase and acquire title to such lease or rental property
within a period not exceeding the useful life of such property and in no case exceeding
30 years. Each such agreement and the terms thereof must be concluded by an ordinance
duly enacted by the city council. The payment of the rentals will be from a general
levy imposed upon both personal and real property included within the boundaries of
the city; by imposing rates, tolls and service charges for the use of such property
or any part thereof by others; from any other available municipal income; or from
any one or more of such sources. The obligation to pay such rentals will not constitute
an indebtedness of the city within the meaning of the constitution and Charter limitations
on contracting on indebtedness. In order to provide necessary equipment for government
operations, including, but not limited to, vehicles or office machines, the city may
enter into short-term leases or rentals which are the result of a competitive procurement
process, or when the purchasing and contracts manager approves the procurement of
such leases under section 4.12.150 .
(Code 2009, § 4.12.540; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2016-59, §
2, 9-27-2016; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
Property acquired or occupied pursuant to this chapter is exempt from taxation so
long as used for authorized governmental or proprietary functions of the city.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.550; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
The city attorney has the ability to maintain an action in any court of competent
jurisdiction to enforce the terms and conditions of any purchase or leasehold agreement
entered into by the city.
(Code 2009, § 4.12.560; Ord. No. O-2011-25, § 2, 4-26-2011; Ord. No. O-2023-03 , § 2, 1-24-2023)
The city funds, exclusive of pension funds, may be invested in any of the following
investment vehicles:
A.
F.D.I.C. insured savings accounts;
B.
Certificates of deposit, F.D.I.C. insured, or secured pursuant to state law;
C.
Securities of the U.S. Government and its agencies or instrumentalities thereof;
D.
Repurchase agreements, secured pursuant to state law;
E.
Prime commercial paper;
F.
Prime bankers' acceptances;
G.
Senior corporate debt obligations;
H.
Local government investment pools pursuant to C.R.S. title 24, article 75, part 7
(C.R.S. § 24-75-701 et seq.);
I.
General or revenue municipal bonds of any state of the United States, the District
of Columbia, or any territorial possession of the United States, or of any political
subdivision, institution, department, agency, instrumentality, or authority of any
such governmental entities;
J.
Money-market funds as allowed by state law.
(Code 1993, § 4.16.010; Code 2009, § 4.16.610; Ord. No. O-92-15, § 1; Ord. No. O-2018-15 , § 1, 4-10-2018; Ord. No. O-2020-34 , § 1, 9-8-2020)
The city council has historically provided and will continue to provide certain public
services through the city's enterprise funds, consistent with all applicable state
laws. Subject to annual grant revenue, which may disqualify enterprises under paragraph
(2)(d) of article X, section 20 of the state constitution (TABOR), the city council:
recognizes and confirms that each enterprise listed below is an "enterprise" within
the meaning of TABOR and, as applicable, C.R.S. title 37, article 45.1 (C.R.S. § 37-45.1-101
et seq.) (the "Enterprise Act"); and declares its intent to operate and maintain each
enterprise to exclude its revenue and its spending and other activities from application
of TABOR. The city enterprises are:
A.
Sanitation enterprise;
B.
Storm drainage enterprise;
C.
Golf enterprise;
D.
Airport enterprise;
E.
Electric and broadband utility enterprise;
F.
Water utility enterprise; and
G.
Wastewater utility enterprise.
(Code 1993, § 4.18.010; Code 2009, § 4.18.010; Ord. No. O-96-01, § 2; Ord. No. O-97-06,
§ 2; Ord. No. O-2013-37, § 1, 8-27-2013)
The city council is the governing board of each enterprise. As such governing board,
the city council shall conduct the business of each enterprise in the same manner
and follow the same procedures as the city council. The city council shall conduct
all official business of each enterprise governing board at regularly scheduled or
special meetings of the city council. The council need not adjourn a council meeting,
or specially convene a governing board meeting to conduct business as such board.
The city clerk shall record the proceedings of the governing board in the minutes
of the city council, and need not record them separately. No additional oath of office,
qualification or procedure shall apply to such service as a member of the governing
board. All otherwise applicable requirements, privileges, immunities, protections,
limitations, and other provisions of law shall apply to and govern all business and
actions of the governing board.
(Code 1993, § 4.18.020; Code 2009, § 4.18.020; Ord. No. O-96-01, § 2)
The city council and the city manager, and their respective designees, acting for
each enterprise, shall exercise all lawful powers, consistent with the Charter, and
ordinances, including, without limitation, the power to issue or reissue bonds, notes,
or other obligations, in the name of the city, payable solely from lawful revenues
available to the enterprise, and to set rates, fees and charges for services provided
by the enterprise. The powers provided in this section shall not modify, limit or
affect the powers conferred by any other law, including ordinances, directly or indirectly.
However, in no event shall the enterprise have authority to levy or collect taxes,
whether sales taxes, use taxes or ad valorem taxes. No enterprise shall have the power
to direct the city or the city council to exercise the city's taxing power for the
enterprise.
(Code 1993, § 4.18.030; Code 2009, § 4.18.030; Ord. No. O-96-01, § 2)
Assets operated and maintained by each enterprise shall remain in the ownership of
the city. The council hereby assigns the use and management of all such assets, real
and personal, to each respective enterprise.
(Code 1993, § 4.18.040; Code 2009, § 4.18.040; Ord. No. O-96-01, § 2)
The council has established a separate enterprise fund to account for revenues and
expenditures of each enterprise. The city manager shall prepare, for each enterprise,
an annual budget, which may be part of the manager's entire budget for the city. All
budgets, reports, audits and financial operations of the enterprise shall conform
to generally accepted accounting principles applicable to governmental units and other
requirements of law.
(Code 1993, § 4.18.050; Code 2009, § 4.18.050; Ord. No. O-96-01, § 2)
Each enterprise hereby assumes all the city obligations incurred for its respective
outstanding revenue bonds, loans and other financial obligations. Each enterprise
hereby ratifies all action taken by the city council in connection with the same.
Nothing in this chapter shall alter the rights of any obligee under such bonds, loans
or other financial obligations.
(Code 1993, § 4.18.060; Code 2009, § 4.18.060; Ord. No. O-96-01, § 2)
The city, under its lawful powers, has incurred general obligation indebtedness for
certain enterprise purposes. The respective enterprises shall continue to pay such
indebtedness from enterprise revenues. However, in no event shall any such debt require
or authorize an enterprise to levy any tax for repayment of such debt. Any such power
or obligation to tax shall be solely that of the city. Nothing in this chapter shall
limit the city's authority, consistent with all other applicable statutes, constitutional
and Charter limitations, to levy taxes for payment of its general obligation indebtedness
now outstanding or any additional general obligation debt the city may, from time
to time, incur, or for any other lawful purpose.
(Code 1993, § 4.18.070; Code 2009, § 4.18.070; Ord. No. O-96-01, § 2)
A special fund is created for the purpose of providing coverage for municipal tort
liability, such special fund being known as the city self-insurance fund; however,
it is subject to the restrictions of liability set forth in the Colorado Governmental
Immunity Act (C.R.S. § 24-10-101 et seq.).
(Code 1981, § 1-3-6(A); Code 1993, § 4.20.010; Code 2009, § 4.20.010)
The fund shall be funded from time to time in such amounts as may be prescribed by
resolution of the city council.
(Code 1981, § 1-3-6(B); Code 1993, § 4.20.020; Code 2009, § 4.20.020)
Payments from the fund for claims against the city for civil liabilities in tort,
payment of insurance premiums insuring the city against such tort liability or otherwise
bonding of such employees against tort liabilities which may be asserted against the
city may be disbursed from the special fund created by section 4.80.010 .
(Code 1981, § 1-3-6(C); Code 1993, § 4.20.030; Code 2009, § 4.20.030)
Pursuant to section 4.20.030 , the city shall purchase and maintain hired and nonowned automobile insurance coverage
for not less than $600,000.00 combined bodily injury and property damage. Such insurance
shall pay any claim arising from the use of a personal vehicle or rental car by any
city employee on city business. Such insurance shall not waive the limits of liability
set forth in the Colorado Governmental Immunity Act. Such insurance is for the sole
benefit of the city and not for city employees or other persons.
(Code 1993, § 4.20.040; Code 2009, § 4.20.040; Ord. No. O-91-27, § 1)
There is created a conservation trust fund for the city.
(Code 1981, § 8-6-1; Code 1993, § 4.24.010; Code 2009, § 4.24.010)
The director of finance is authorized and directed to deposit all funds received from
the conservation trust fund of the state into the conservation trust fund of the city.
(Code 1981, § 8-6-2; Code 1993, § 4.24.020; Code 2009, § 4.24.020)
All funds deposited in the conservation trust fund shall be expended only for the
acquisition, development and maintenance of new conservation sites as defined in C.R.S.
§ 29-21-101.
(Code 1981, § 8-6-3; Code 1993, § 4.24.030; Code 2009, § 4.24.030)
There is established a park fund for the city for the purchase of lands for additional
parks of the city and improvement thereof.
(Code 1981, § 8-5-1; Code 1993, § 4.32.010; Code 2009, § 4.32.010)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the fleet service fund, for the purpose of affecting centralized
control over the acquisition, maintenance, allocation and disposal of all city-owned
motor vehicles and motorized equipment.
(Code 1993, § 4.36.010; Code 2009, § 4.36.010; Ord. No. O-82-79, § 1)
The fleet service fund shall be funded through the establishment of a fleet lease
charge, designated for each such motorized unit by the fleet manager of the city,
calculated on the basis of replacement cost, service life, maintenance and operational
costs. The fleet manager shall annually, in conjunction with the budget process of
the city, revise the fleet lease charges as may be necessary, to reflect charges in
economic conditions in capital and operational expenses.
(Code 1993, § 4.36.020; Code 2009, § 4.36.020; Ord. No. O-82-79, § 2)
The annual budget for each department and division thereof using city motor vehicles
and equipment shall designate, as a specific line item, the projected cost of motor
vehicle and equipment usage applicable to it, based on the fleet lease charge, said
allocated money to be transferred to the fleet service fund.
(Code 1993, § 4.36.030; Code 2009, § 4.36.030; Ord. No. O-82-79, § 3)
All motor vehicles and equipment heretofore having been designated as an asset of
the electric fund, water fund, sewer fund and general fund, shall be designated as
an asset of the fleet service fund.
(Code 1993, § 4.36.040; Code 2009, § 4.36.040; Ord. No. O-82-79, § 4)
Pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, the special museum trust fund shall receive all specially designated
trusts, bequests and endowments given for the use of the Longmont Museum. This fund
shall be an interest-bearing account and any interest earned shall be credited to
this fund.
(Code 1993, § 4.40.010; Code 2009, § 4.40.010; Ord. No. O-82-81, § 1; Ord. No. O-94-88,
§ 2)
The director of finance or designee, in conjunction with the recommendations of the
Longmont Museum Advisory Board, shall prepare a proposed budget of the special museum
trust fund for the ensuing fiscal year to be presented by the city manager upon or
before September 1 of each year in conjunction with the general fund budget. The city
council, no later than November 1, shall adopt a budget for the special museum trust
fund and provide for the annual appropriations therefrom.
(Code 1993, § 4.40.030; Code 2009, § 4.40.030; Ord. No. O-82-81, § 3; Ord. No. O-90-85,
§ 2)
The special museum trust fund shall be subject to appropriations therefrom and be
budgeted to reflect expenditures for the exclusive use of the Longmont Museum for
expenditures beyond the scope of the museum general fund budget.
(Code 1993, § 4.40.040; Code 2009, § 4.40.040; Ord. No. O-82-81, § 4; Ord. No. O-90-85,
§ 3)
Editor's note— Formerly entitled "Museum Services Fund," which was renamed by Ord. No. O-2018-58 , as herein set out.
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the museum grants, donations and services fund for the purpose
of receiving funds donated or granted to the city museum and providing city museum
services. The fund will serve as the recipient of the scientific and cultural facilities
district (SCFD) funding. The city council shall at any time have authority to make
appropriations from the fund. The funds and any earned interest are for the specific
use of the Longmont Museum.
(Code 1993, § 4.41.010; Code 2009, § 4.41.010; Ord. No. O-94-88, § 1; Ord. No. O-2018-58 , § 3, 12-11-2018)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the library services fund for the purpose of receiving funds
donated or granted to the city public library. The city council shall at any time
have authority to make appropriations from the fund. The funds and any earned interest
are for the specific use of the city public library.
(Code 1993, § 4.42.010; Code 2009, § 4.42.010; Ord. No. O-94-87, § 1)
Per section 9.9 of the Charter, there is created a special fund, known as the Callahan House Fund,
to receive funds earned by, paid to, donated or granted to the Callahan House. The
city council has authority to make appropriations from the fund. The funds and any
earned interest are for the specific use and benefit of the Callahan House.
(Code 1993, § 4.43.010; Code 2009, § 4.43.010; Ord. No. O-95-17, § 3)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the sanitation enterprise fund, for the purpose of identifying
and controlling all revenues and expenses attributable to municipal sanitation services.
(Code 1993, § 4.44.010; Code 2009, § 4.44.010; Ord. No. O-84-51, § 1)
The sanitation enterprise fund shall be funded through revenues derived from the collection
of landfill user fees, residential refuse collection charges, trash container fees,
and such other sanitation-related fees and charges as the council may from time to
time establish.
(Code 1993, § 4.44.020; Code 2009, § 4.44.020; Ord. No. O-84-51, § 2)
The establishment of the fund shall not serve as any limitation to the combined billing
procedures relating to municipal utilities and services provided by the city.
(Code 1993, § 4.44.030; Code 2009, § 4.40.030; Ord. No. O-84-51, § 3)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the senior services fund, for the purpose of receiving funds
donated or granted to the city senior services. The city council shall at any time
have authority to make appropriations from the fund. The funds and any earned interest
are for the specific use and benefit of the city senior services.
(Code 1993, § 4.45.010; Code 2009, § 4.45.010; Ord. No. O-95-30, § 1)
Pursuant to the authority of section 9.9 of the Charter, there is established a special fund designated as the downtown parking
special revenue fund, for the purpose of, after the payment of all associated administrative
costs incurred, financing public capital improvements of, and within the territorial
jurisdiction of the city downtown development authority, which capital improvements
are approved by the city council.
(Code 1993, § 4.48.010; Code 2009, § 4.48.010; Ord. No. O-84-69, § 1; Ord. No. O-93-28,
§ 1)
The fund shall be funded by the net revenues derived from parking fees collected from
the sale of parking permits as set forth in chapter 11.36 . For the purpose of this chapter, the term "net revenues" shall be defined as gross
revenues minus payment of all associated administrative costs incurred by the city
manager or program designee.
(Code 1993, § 4.48.020; Code 2009, § 4.48.020; Ord. No. O-84-69, § 2; Ord. No. O-93-28,
§ 2)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the storm drainage enterprise fund, for the purpose of identifying
and controlling all revenues and expenses attributable to municipal storm drainage
services.
(Code 1993, § 4.52.010; Code 2009, § 4.52.010; Ord. No. O-84-84, § 1)
The storm drainage enterprise fund shall be funded through revenues derived from the
collection of storm drainage user fees, and such other storm drainage related fees
and charges, and other appropriations from the general and public improvement funds
as the council may from time to time establish.
(Code 1993, § 4.52.020; Code 2009, § 4.52.020; Ord. No. O-84-84, § 2)
The establishment of this fund shall not serve as any limitation to the combined billing
procedures relating to municipal utilities and services otherwise provided by the
city.
(Code 1993, § 4.52.030; Code 2009, § 4.52.030; Ord. No. O-84-84, § 3)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the community development block grant program fund, for
the purpose of deposit of block grant funds of the federal government, pursuant to
title I of the Housing and Community Development Act of 1974, and the disbursal of
monies therefrom in accordance with the stated and approved project goals, and to
otherwise achieve compliance with all applicable federal laws and regulations.
(Code 1993, § 4.56.010; Code 2009, § 4.56.010; Ord. No. O-84-97, § 1)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the golf enterprise fund, for the purpose of identifying
and controlling all revenues and expenses attributable to the operation of the city
municipal golf courses.
(Code 1993, § 4.60.010; Code 2009, § 4.60.010; Ord. No. O-84-98, § 1)
The golf enterprise fund shall be funded through all revenues designated by the city
council, including green fees, season passes, commissions from concessionaire sales,
locker rentals and cart fees.
(Code 1993, § 4.60.020; Code 2009, § 4.60.020; Ord. No. O-84-98, § 2)

D.
In this section, the term "junior" means a person who is 18 years old or younger.
The term "senior" means a person who is 60 years to 74 years old. The term "super
