through Friday. Weekday fees are not valid on city holidays.
The city manager or designee may authorize temporary waivers or changes to the above
fee structure to promote golfing and charitable events.
(Code 1993, § 4.60.030; Code 2009, § 4.60.030; Ord. No. O-93-06, § 1; Ord. No. O-94-05,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-94-66, § 1; Ord. No. O-95-20, § 1; Ord. No. O-96-71, § 1; Ord. No.
O-97-41, § 1; Ord. No. O-97-90, § 1; Ord. No. O-98-65, § 1; Ord. No. O-99-80, § 1;
Ord. No. O-2000-55, § 1; Ord. No. O-2001-63, § 1; Ord. No. O-2002-55, § 1; Ord. No.
O-2003-72, § 1; Ord. No. O-2004-81, § 1; Ord. No. O-2005-87, § 1; Ord. No. O-2006-92,
§ 1(4.60.030); Ord. No. O-2007-95, § 1; Ord. No. 2008-91, § 1; Ord. No. O-2010-46,
§ 1, 10-26-2010; Ord. No. O-2014-60, § 1, 10-28-2014)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the airport enterprise fund, for the purpose of identifying
and controlling all revenues and expenses attributable to the operation of the Vance
Brand Airport, Longmont, Colorado.
(Code 1993, § 4.64.010; Code 2009, § 4.64.010; Ord. No. O-86-70, § 1; Ord. No. O-88-67,
§ 3)
The airport enterprise fund shall be funded through all revenues designated by the
city council, including aircraft fees and taxes, airport space leases, and an airport
fuel use charge.
(Code 1993, § 4.64.020; Code 2009, § 4.64.020; Ord. No. O-86-70, § 2)
All commercial and noncommercial leases granted by the city at the Vance Brand Municipal
Airport shall require a lease contract with specified terms, conditions and rates.
The lease rate shall be governed by this section of this Code.
A.
All lease rates shall be as established in the most current version of the airport's
schedule of rates and charges approved by resolution of the city council. Each lease
contract shall be subject to city council approval through review, adoption, and execution
of the lease contract.
B.
The lease rate for all leases shall be automatically adjusted annually based on the
changes in the previous calendar year's Bureau of Labor Statistics Denver, Boulder/Greeley
All Urban Consumer Price Index, all categories (CPI). Such adjustments shall reflect
the percentage increase added to the base lease rate; however, in years that the CPI
decreases, the adjustment to the annual lease rate shall be zero. Each year, the airport
manager shall notify each leaseholder of the new lease rate at least 30 days in advance
of the anniversary of the lease.
C.
When the city manager or his designee, or the airport manager, determines a market
analysis of rates for the airport is needed, the airport manager may perform such
analysis in order to determine a new base rate for leases at the airport.
D.
The airport's schedule of rates and charges shall be reviewed annually by the airport
manager to determine if market adjustments are recommended. The airport manager shall
forward any recommended adjustments to the city council, which may approve, reject,
or modify the adjustments by resolution.
E.
Prior to submission of a recommendation to city council for changes to the airport's
schedule of rates and charges, lessees at the airport shall be notified at least 30
days in advance of the submission to city council. The notification to tenants shall
be made by posting it on the city's airport website.
F.
The fuel flowage fee for the airport shall be established by council as set forth
in the airport's schedule of rates and charges.
(Code 1993, § 4.64.030; Code 2009, § 4.64.030; Ord. No. O-93-07; Ord. No. O-95-112,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-96-13, § 1; Ord. No. O-96-56, § 1; Ord. No. O-99-75, § 1; Ord. No.
O-2006-49, § 1; Ord. No. O-2013-46, § 1, 10-22-2013; Ord. No. O-2016-39, § 1, 5-24-2016; Ord. No. O-2026-16 , § 2, 4-14-2026)
A.
Purpose. This section implements section 12.7 of the Charter by permitting the temporary use of public places. Any uses subject
to chapter 13.37 shall be governed by chapter 13.37 and shall not be subject to this section.
B.
Public use areas. The airport manager may designate areas at the airport for use by the public, including,
but not limited to, skydive operators, skydivers, ultralight aircraft, sport aircraft,
powered parachutes, hot air balloons, aircraft or motor vehicle parking and other
uses deemed reasonable by the airport manager and consistent with general aviation
operations. Parachute landing areas (drop zones) shall be designated by the airport
manager.
C.
Permit required. No person may use the public use areas without first obtaining a permit from the
airport manager and paying the applicable permit fee.
D.
Permit fee. The permit fee shall be as established in the most current version of the airport's
schedule of rates and charges, as approved by resolution of the city council.
E.
Annual permits for lessees. The purpose of this subsection is to foster a business-friendly environment by providing
lessees the opportunity reasonably to predict their future costs and to provide the
city the opportunity to receive increased revenues. Any person with a hangar lease,
a specialized aviation service operator lease, or a fixed based operator lease who
receives an annual permit shall be subject to this subsection. The annual permit may
be renewed each year, but is subject to annual adjustment based on the previous calendar
year's Bureau of Labor Statistics Denver/Boulder/Greeley All Urban Consumer Price
Index, all categories for the Denver/Boulder region. Such adjustments shall reflect
the percentage increase added to the permit fee; however, in years that the CPI decreases,
the adjustment to the permit fee shall be zero.
F.
Exceptions. For special events hosted by the Vance Brand Municipal Airport, including, but not
limited to, airshows, fly-in events, and other similar events, the airport manager
may negotiate different rates through an agreement which may be approved by the city
manager or his designee. Without limiting the foregoing, if there is a civic organization
or not-for-profit organization in which aviation is being promoted or is a substantial
part of the event, the airport manager may waive or reduce the daily or weekly permit
fee, after approval by the city manager or his designee.
(Code 2009, § 4.64.040; Ord. No. O-2007-89, § 1; Ord. No. O-2016-39, § 2, 5-24-2016)
There is created a youth services special revenue fund, a special fund organized pursuant
to section 9.9 of the Charter.
(Code 1993, § 4.68.010; Code 2009, § 4.68.010; Ord. No. O-87-27, § 1)
The youth services special revenue fund shall serve as a vehicle for the receipt and
deposit of all specially designated gifts, donations, bequests, endowments and other
monies specifically earmarked for the use and benefit of the city youth services.
(Code 1993, § 4.68.020; Code 2009, § 4.68.020; Ord. No. O-87-27, § 2)
The director of finance or designee shall prepare a proposed budget of the fund for
the ensuing fiscal year to be presented by the city manager upon or before September
1 of each year in conjunction with the general fund budget. The city council no later
than November 1 shall adopt a budget for the fund and provide for the annual appropriations
therefrom. The council shall at any time have authority to make additional appropriations
from the fund for unanticipated expenditures.
(Code 1993, § 4.68.030; Code 2009, § 4.68.030; Ord. No. O-87-27, § 3)
The youth services special revenue fund shall be subject to appropriations therefrom
and be budgeted to reflect expenditures for the exclusive use of city youth services
for expenditures beyond the scope of the youth services budget.
(Code 1993, § 4.68.040; Code 2009, § 4.68.040; Ord. No. O-87-27, § 4)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the parks grants and donations fund for the purpose of receiving
funds donated or granted to the city for the development or improvement of parks.
The city council shall at any time have authority to make appropriations from the
fund.
(Code 1993, § 4.69.010; Code 2009, § 4.69.010; Ord. No. O-96-26, § 1)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the park and greenway maintenance fund for the purposes
described at section 14.50.010 . The city council shall at any time have authority to make appropriations from the
fund as restricted by chapter 14.50 .
(Code 2009, § 4.70.010; Ord. No. O-2013-63, § 1, 11-12-2013)
The park and greenway maintenance fund shall be funded through revenues derived from
collection of the park and greenway maintenance fee pursuant to chapter 14.50 .
(Code 2009, § 4.70.020; Ord. No. O-2013-63, § 1, 11-12-2013)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the prairie dog habitat restoration fund for the purposes
described at section 7.06.080.B. The city council shall at any time have authority
to make appropriations from the fund as restricted by chapter 7.06 .
(Code 2009, § 4.71.010; Ord. No. O-2019-01 , § 3, 1-8-2019)
The prairie dog habitat restoration fund shall be funded through revenues derived
from collection of the prairie dog habitat restoration fee pursuant to section 7.06.080.B.
(Code 2009, § 4.71.020; Ord. No. O-2019-01 , § 3, 1-8-2019)
The council of the city finds:
A.
The development of certain commercial and industrial facilities is in the public interest,
and the encouragement thereof is a vital and important measure and public purpose
to promote economic development in the city.
B.
An enhanced development incentive program that includes commercial facilities, both
retail and office facilities, within the city's downtown development authority area,
the Southeast Longmont Urban Renewal Area and along Main Street, and industrial facilities
city-wide will promote the guiding principles, goals and policies of the city area
comprehensive plan including livable centers, corridors, and neighborhoods; and job
growth and economic vitality through innovation and collaboration.
C.
A fee rebate program for nonprofit human service delivery and governmental agencies
will promote the guiding principles, goals and policies of the city area comprehensive
plan including livable centers, corridors, and neighborhoods; responsible stewardship
of our resources; and housing, services, amenities and opportunities for all.
D.
A rebate of the transportation community investment fee for commercial and industrial
in-fill development and redevelopment will promote the guiding principles, goals and
policies of the city area comprehensive plan including livable centers, corridors,
and neighborhoods; and a complete, balanced and connected transportation system.
E.
A fee rebate program for commercial development projects primarily benefiting the
youth of the city will promote the guiding principles, goals and policies of the city
area comprehensive plan including housing, services, amenities, and opportunities
for all; and a safe, healthy, and adaptable community.
(Code 1993, § 4.72.005; Code 2009, § 4.72.005; Ord. No. O-89-43, § 1; Ord. No. O-93-69,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-96-84, § 1; Ord. No. O-98-25, § 1; Ord. No. O-99-47, § 1; Ord. No.
O-2004-28, § 1; Ord. No. O-2005-90, § 2; Ord. No. O-2008-73, § 1; Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 9, 8-14-2018)
A.
Commercial businesses; expiration. Any sole proprietorship, partnership or corporation that, on or after February 1,
2008, locates a new, or expands an existing, commercial business within the city's
downtown development authority area and the area encompassed by one block east and
one block west of Main Street from Longs Peak Avenue north to 11th Avenue shall be
eligible to apply for a rebate of the applicable development fees. The rebates to
commercial businesses authorized by this subsection shall expire and be of no force
or effect on November 1, 2011, unless extended by a legislative act of the city council.
B.
Non-commercial businesses. Any sole proprietorship, partnership or corporation that locates a new, or expands
an existing noncommercial business or industry within the city limits shall be eligible
to apply for a rebate of the applicable development fees.
C.
Clarification of terms. For purposes of this section, expansion of an existing business or industry does
not include any corporate reorganization, sale of an existing business or resumption
of business activities unless such business has been closed for at least the previous
24 months.
(Code 1993, § 4.72.010; Code 2009, § 4.72.010; Ord. No. O-87-87, § 1; Ord. No. O-2001-21,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2002-03, § 1; Ord. No. O-2005-90, § 3; Ord. No. O-2008-73, § 2; Ord.
No. O-2009-22, § 1, 5-12-2009)
Any nonprofit human service delivery agency registered as a nonprofit corporation
in the state, with an Internal Revenue Service 501(C)(3) designation, locating a new
facility or expanding an existing facility within the city, shall be eligible to apply
for a rebate of the otherwise applicable development fees.
(Code 1993, § 4.72.015; Code 2009, § 4.72.015; Ord. No. O-96-84, § 2; Ord. No. O-2005-90,
§ 4)
Any vacant site within a building area that is within the city boundaries as they
existed prior to 1980, and which is undeveloped or subject to redevelopment, that
is, replacement of, or more than a 50 percent addition to, an existing building, is
eligible to apply for a rebate of the otherwise applicable transportation community
investment fee.
(Code 1993, § 4.72.016; Code 2009, § 4.72.016; Ord. No. O-98-25, § 2; Ord. No. O-2005-90,
§ 5)
The following fees shall be eligible for rebate or waiver under this chapter:
A.
The building permit and plan check fees set forth in chapter 16.04 ;
B.
Electrical permit fees set forth in chapter 16.08 ;
C.
Mechanical permit fees set forth in chapter 16.12 ;
D.
Plumbing permit fees set forth in section 16.16.070 ;
E.
Water meter installation fee set forth in chapter 14.04 ;
F.
City sales tax, as required by chapter 4.04 ;
G.
Transportation community investment fee set forth in chapter 14.38 (in-fill properties only).
(Code 1993, § 4.72.020; Code 2009, § 4.72.020; Ord. No. O-87-87, § 1; Ord. No. O-93-69,
§ 2; Ord. No. O-97-19, § 1; Ord. No. O-98-25, § 3; Ord. No. O-2001-21, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2002-03, § 2; Ord. No. O-2004-28, § 2; Ord. No. O-2005-90, § 6; Ord. No. O-2015-17,
§ 4, 3-17-2015)
A.
Each new or expanding business may apply for a rebate of the fees described in section 4.72.020 , using the forms supplied by the director of planning and development services. The
director may rebate up to a maximum of 30 percent of each fee, when a certificate
of occupancy is issued, and shall base the amount of rebated fees upon the following
factors:
1.
The number of jobs created by the business;
2.
Whether the business is within the downtown development authority district;
3.
Whether the project will commence within six months of the rebate of fee approval;
4.
Whether the project meets the goals and policies of the city area comprehensive plan;
5.
Whether the business addresses retail leakage as identified in the city retail opportunities
study or in information provided by the applicant that the city will derive net new
sales tax by virtue of the business;
6.
Whether the business implements energy conservation goals by either exceeding the
minimum standards in the city's energy code or by pursuing LEEDS building certification;
7.
The value of the building's construction expanded or remodeled; and
8.
Other appropriate factors that the director of planning and development services may
from time to time set forth in an administrative regulation.
B.
Each nonprofit human service delivery agency may apply for fee rebates, with a building
permit application, using the forms supplied by the director of planning and development
services. The director shall base the amount of fees rebated upon the following factors:
1.
Whether the nonprofit agency provides human services in one of the following categories:
a.
Services that provide for basic emergency needs, such as food, shelter and clothing;
b.
Services that offer protection from abuse and neglect, such as child and adult protection
and domestic violence intervention services;
c.
Prevention and intervention services, such as school dropout prevention, mental health
and substance abuse counseling; or
d.
Services that promote independence and self-sufficiency, such as employment training,
independent living skills training, transportation and in-home services.
2.
Whether the nonprofit agency demonstrates it provides most of its services to city
residents with incomes of less than 50 percent of the area median income as set by
the U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development;
3.
Whether the nonprofit agency demonstrates its development project will significantly
increase the number of city residents receiving the human services stated in subsection
B.1 of this section;
4.
Whether the nonprofit agency demonstrates through its financial statements, or other
appropriate documentation, it can only accomplish the project with the rebate of fees;
and
5.
Other appropriate factors consistent with the purposes of section 4.72.005.C the director
of planning and development services may from time to time set forth in an administrative
regulation.
C.
Each owner of an in-fill property may apply for a rebate of the transportation community
investment fee, along with a building permit application, using the forms supplied
by the director of planning and development services. The director shall base the
amount of fees rebated upon the following factors:
1.
Any in-fill project may be eligible for a rebate of up to 50 percent of the transportation
community investment fee.
2.
In-fill projects which demonstrate that they will generate no new or additional traffic,
as determined by a traffic study acceptable to the city traffic engineer, may be eligible
for an additional rebate of up to 100 percent of the transportation community investment
fee.
D.
Each application shall contain the name and address of the property owner, the developer,
a description of the project and such supporting characteristics deemed necessary
by the director of planning and development services and the applicant.
E.
Each owner of a commercial development project primarily benefiting the youth of the
city may apply for a rebate of fees, along with a building permit application, using
the forms supplied by the director of planning and development services. The director
shall base the amount of fees waived upon the following factors:
1.
The extent to which the applicant can demonstrate that the commercial development
is intended and capable of primarily benefiting youth in the community (e.g., youth
activity center, youth nightclub).
2.
Special consideration will be given to projects identified by the city's youth council.
3.
The fees eligible to be waived administratively are identified in section 4.72.020 ; additional fees may be waived by the city council.
(Code 1993, § 4.72.030; Code 2009, § 4.72.030; Ord. No. O-87-87, § 1; Ord. No. O-96-84,
§ 3; Ord. No. O-98-25, § 4; Ord. No. O-99-47, § 2; Ord. No. O-2001-21, § 3; Ord. No.
O-2002-03, § 3; Ord. No. O-2005-90, § 7; Ord. No. O-2008-73, § 3; Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 9, 8-14-2018)
A.
Besides the fee rebates provided in the preceding sections of this chapter, the city
council may, by ordinance, adopted by a two-thirds majority of the entire membership
thereof, in its sole discretion, agree to rebate some or all of the fees and development
requirements specified in subsection B of this section for any development application
if it finds, in its sole discretion, that such additional rebate is in the interest
of the health, safety and welfare of the citizens of the city and serves the vital
and important public purposes enumerated in section 4.72.005 . In evaluating the proposed enhanced fee rebate, the council shall, in addition to
the factors enumerated above, consider the extent to which:
1.
There is a probability of expansion with additional employees and additional construction
within the five years immediately following the proposed rebate;
2.
The development will, through extension of public infrastructure necessary to serve
it, also facilitate annexation and development of other new projects to the benefit
of the economic health and vitality of the city; and
3.
The development qualifies for and will receive available county, state or federal
economic development incentives.
B.
Some or all of the following additional fees and development requirements may be rebated,
in whole or in part:
1.
Up to 100 percent of the fees eligible for rebate under section 4.72.020 ;
2.
The municipal subdistrict of the Northern Colorado Water Conservation District surcharge,
established in section 14.04.210 ; and
3.
The development application fees established pursuant to title 15 .
C.
The rebates provided above shall, if granted by the city council, be reduced to a
written agreement with the developer and shall be subject to such terms and conditions
as will, in the judgment of the council, ensure that the eligibility criteria and
other pertinent development conditions will be performed and satisfied. If any such
terms or conditions are not performed or satisfied, the rebates provided for in this
section may be revoked and the development and developer shall be subject to all the
fees and requirements that would, but for the rebates granted pursuant to this section,
have been applied.
(Code 1993, § 4.72.040; Code 2009, § 4.72.040; Ord. No. O-89-43, § 2; Ord. No. O-2001-21,
§ 4; Ord. No. O-2001-78, § 3; Ord. No. O-2002-03, § 4; Ord. No. O-2005-90, § 8; Ord.
No. O-2008-73, § 4; Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 9, 8-14-2018)
A.
Businesses eligible for consideration. Any sole proprietorship, partnership or corporation that locates a new business,
or expands its existing business within the city limits creating higher paying primary
jobs shall be eligible to apply for economic development incentives. New or expanding
business shall not include any corporate reorganization, sale of an existing business
or resumption of business activities unless such business has been closed for at least
the previous 24 months. For purposes of this chapter, primary job means employment
with a company which sells the majority of its services or products outside Boulder
County.
B.
Economic development incentive requirements and criteria. Approval of an application for a development incentive requires that the applicant
agree to increase its number of primary jobs by at least ten new jobs at an average
wage level at least five percent above the average wage for the city, to complete
the primary job expansion within two years of the date set out in the agreement for
incentive approval, and to maintain the number of newly created primary jobs for the
full length of the incentive period without a reduction in total employment. For applicants
receiving either state or county economic development incentives for the project under
consideration, the required average wage level shall be at least five percent above
the average wage for the county where the business is located, instead of five percent
above the average wage for the city.
C.
Economic development incentive amounts.
1.
The city manager or his designee may approve all fee/tax rebate requests of up to
$75,000.00. Any request that exceeds the staff approval guidelines must be approved
by the city council.
2.
The city manager or his designee may grant an applicant meeting the requirements and
criteria of this section an incentive equal to 50 percent of the increase of its business
personal property taxes, over the amount assessed in the calendar year in which the
personal property for the incentivized project is acquired and first put into use,
the base year. The base year and the amount of business personal property tax assessed
in the base year shall remain fixed throughout the incentive period. The incentive
payment shall be rebated in each of the four consecutive years beginning with one
of the three calendars years immediately following the base year. The applicant shall
notify the city in writing at least six months prior to January 1 of the year it wants
the incentive payments to commence. The amount of the incentive payment shall be determined
by the amount the applicant's business personal property taxes assessed and paid in
each of those years exceed the business personal property taxes assessed in the base
year.
3.
A primary employer exceeding the requirement and criteria may be eligible for an enhanced
incentive, exceeding the 50 percent limit and four-year period stated above, as determined
by the city council. The city council may, by ordinance, in its sole discretion, enhance
the incentive percentage and extend the incentive period up to eight years if, and
to the extent that it finds that the additional payment is in the interest of the
health, safety and welfare of the citizens of the city and serves the vital and important
public purposes enumerated in section 4.72.005 . In evaluating the enhanced incentive percentage and period, the council shall, in
addition to the factors enumerated above, consider the extent to which:
a.
The applicant will actually expand primary jobs above the minimum level stated above
within the extended incentive period.
b.
The applicant may actually relocate from the city, or may actually choose to locate
in another city.
c.
The applicant qualifies for and will receive available county, state or federal economic
development incentives.
d.
The applicant's project is consistent with the target industries and goals identified
in Advance Longmont, as amended, or any successor document.
D.
Incentive agreement. The economic development incentive payments provided in subsections C and F of this
section shall be reduced to a written agreement with the employer ensuring that the
respective criteria will be met within two years from the date of the incentive agreement
and shall be subject to the business being current on all obligations to the city
and to such other terms and conditions as will, in the judgment of the city, ensure
that the eligibility criteria and other pertinent development conditions will be performed
and satisfied. If any such terms or conditions are not performed or satisfied, the
agreement shall terminate and the employer shall not be eligible for any further incentive
payments.
E.
Rebate of fees. As an alternative to an economic development incentive payment, an applicant that
qualifies under section 4.72.010 may elect to receive a rebate of permit and development fees under section 4.72.030.A.
In no instance may an employer receive both economic incentive payments and rebate
of fees, unless expressly authorized by city council by ordinance in accordance with
the criteria set forth in subsection C.3 of this section.
F.
Incentive payments for increased employment.
1.
In addition to the incentive under subsection C of this section, an applicant may
apply for an incentive payment equal to the net increase in the unrestricted portion
of combined city sales and use taxes paid, as herein described, using the forms supplied
by the director of economic development. The finance director, or designee, shall
grant one such application to an applicant meeting the requirements and criteria of
this section. After granting such application, the finance director, or designee,
shall make up to three annual payments equal to 100 percent of the applicant's net
increase in the unrestricted portion of combined city sales and use taxes paid on
purchases of measurement and inspection equipment and research and development equipment
over the average net annual unrestricted portion of use taxes due or paid to the city
by the applicant and any business it has purchased or merged with during the 36 months
or lesser period immediately preceding the initial expansion date identified in the
application, the base period. The increase in sales and use taxes will be adjusted
if necessary to eliminate the effect of any change in the tax rate between the base
period and the period of increased employment.
2.
The finance director, or designee, shall make payments equal to the net increase in
such unrestricted portion of combined sales and use taxes paid in each of the three
consecutive years, beginning as of the initial expansion date, for each year the applicant
maintains the number of newly created primary jobs required by subsection B of this
section. Such payment shall be the amount by which the applicant's unrestricted portion
of combined sales and use taxes paid on purchases of measurement and inspection equipment
and research and development equipment in each of those years exceed the average net
annual unrestricted portion of use taxes due or paid during the base period. Applicant
must submit proof of payment of taxes for each payment year within 45 days after each
of the three 12-month periods following the initial expansion date. The city will
make each year's payment within 180 days of receipt of proof of payment.
3.
To qualify for an incentive payment under this section, the applicant must meet and
maintain the job creation requirements of subsection B of this section for each year
in which an incentive payment is claimed.
4.
The applicant shall document, to the finance director, or designee's satisfaction,
measurement and inspection and research and development purchases and the creation
and maintenance of jobs required by subsection B of this section.
5.
The first two years of the applicant's operation in the city may not constitute any
part of a base period. In the absence of 36 full months of operation, the average
annual use tax paid will be based on the months between the end of the first two years
of operation and the initial expansion date.
6.
Payments under this subsection F are authorized for not more than the three-year period
described in this subsection F, unless expanded by authorization of city council,
upon a finding in the council's sole discretion, that such additional incentive payments
are in the interest of the health, safety and welfare of the citizens of the city
and serves the vital and important public purposes enumerated in section 4.72.005 .
7.
As an alternative to using the average annual use taxes paid during the base period,
the applicant may require the finance director, or designee, to determine the actual
sales and use taxes due or paid on purchases of measurement and inspection equipment
and research and development equipment for the purpose of determining sales and use
taxes due or paid during the base period. The finance director, or designee, shall
make such a determination through an audit of applicant's records at applicant's cost.
8.
In order to determine an accurate average annual use tax for the base period, the
finance director, or designee, may calculate the actual sales and use taxes due by
an applicant on purchases of measurement and inspection equipment and research and
development equipment during the base period. The finance director, or designee, shall
make such a determination through an audit of applicant's records at the city's cost.
9.
As used in this subsection F:
a.
Initial expansion date means the date that the applicant expects to begin the expansion,
i.e., the date set out in the agreement for incentive approval identified in subsection
B of this section;
b.
The term "research and development equipment" and "measurement and inspection equipment"
have the definitions given those terms in section 4.04.020 ; and
c.
The unrestricted portion of sales and use taxes is that portion not dedicated to special
funds, such as street system maintenance and improvements fund; open space sales tax
fund; and the public safety sales tax fund.
(Code 1993, § 4.72.045; Code 2009, § 4.72.045; Ord. No. O-2005-09, § 9; Ord. No. O-2007-29,
§ 1; Ord. No. O-2008-73, § 5; Ord. No. O-2012-61, § 1, 10-9-2012; Ord. No. O-2016-57,
§ 2, 9-13-2016; Ord. No. O-2017-04, § 2, 1-31-2017)
Any decision by the director of community development under this chapter or the applicable
administrative regulations may be appealed to the city manager. Such appeal shall
be made in writing no later than ten days after the decision of the director of community
development.
(Code 1993, § 4.72.050; Code 2009, § 4.72.050; Ord. No. O-87-87, § 1; Ord. No. O-2005-90,
§ 10)
The city manager shall, no later than December 31 of each year, report to the council
the results of the operation of this chapter in terms of the costs and benefits to
the city in terms of new businesses attracted to the city, the economic benefits therefrom
and the financial impact upon the governmental and other operations of the city. This
material shall be presented to the council to facilitate its annual determination
as to whether this chapter shall be repealed or amended.
(Code 1993, § 4.72.060; Code 2009, § 4.72.060; Ord. No. O-89-64, § 1; Ord. No. O-2001-21,
§ 5)
To encourage the location of governmental services and employment opportunities in
the city, the city manager or designee shall waive the collection of building, electrical,
mechanical, plumbing and fire code permit and plan check fees set out in title 16 , if the applicant is the United States, the state or any of their departments, agencies
or political subdivisions.
(Code 1993, § 4.72.070; Code 2009, § 4.72.070; Ord. No. O-2004-28, § 3)
The following fees for all new construction within the city, as defined in this Code,
shall be paid at the time a building permit is issued:
A.
Building permit and plan check fees;
B.
Electrical permit fee;
C.
Plumbing permit fee;
D.
Mechanical permit fee;
E.
Electrical connection fee;
F.
Sewer inspection fee; and
G.
City and county sales and use taxes.
(Code 1993, § 4.78.010; Code 2009, § 4.78.010; Ord. No. O-2004-29, § 1; Ord. No. O-2019-18 , § 2, 3-19-2019)
The following fees for all new construction within the city, as defined in this Code,
shall be paid at the time a building permit is issued, except that for residential
construction they need not be paid until the time a permanent or temporary certificate
of occupancy is issued:
A.
The water system development fee;
B.
The sewer system development fee;
C.
The surcharge on water fees for payment of Windy Gap costs;
D.
The electrical community investment fee, unless previously paid to LPC pursuant to
section 14.32.240;
E.
The recreation buildings impact fee;
F.
The transportation impact and street improvement fees;
G.
The park improvement fee;
H.
The storm drainage system capital improvement fee; and
I.
The solid waste/recycling center fee.
(Code 2009, § 4.78.020; Ord. No. O-2019-18 , § 2, 3-19-2019; Ord. No. O-2024-56 , § 7, 8-27-2024)
The council will annually review the schedule provided in this chapter.
(Code 2009, § 4.78.030; Ord. No. O-2019-18 , § 2, 3-19-2019)
Any person, sole proprietorship, partnership, corporation or nonprofit organization
who wishes to construct affordable housing, as defined by this Code, or attainable
housing defined in subsection 4.79.030.C, below, within the city limits shall be eligible
to apply for a reduction or subsidy of the applicable development fees for the affordable
and attainable homes being provided. Commercial structures, garages or other outbuildings
are not eligible under this section, but may be eligible under other provisions of
this Code.
(Code 1993, § 4.79.010; Code 2009, § 4.79.010; Ord. No. O-92-65, § 1; Ord. No. O-95-05,
§ 2; Ord. No. O-2002-17, § 1; Ord. No. O-2021-19 , § 2, 4-13-2021; Ord. No. O-2023-59 , § 2, 11-14-2023)
A.
The following fees shall be eligible for reduction or subsidy under this chapter:
1.
Building permit;
2.
Electric permit;
3.
Plumbing permit;
4.
Heating permit;
5.
Gas permit;
6.
Tapping materials fee;
7.
Electrical connection fee;
8.
Polycart escrow fee;
9.
Parks improvement fee;
10.
Storm drainage fee;
11.
Street improvement fee;
12.
Sprinkler inspection fee;
13.
Plan check fee;
14.
The unrestricted portion of city sales tax;
15.
Recreation buildings impact fees;
16.
Transportation impact fees;
17.
Sewer inspection fee;
18.
Water meter installation fee; and
19.
Water pit inspection fees.
B.
Subject to the appropriation of funds from other than the water, sewer and electric
and broadband utility funds and satisfaction of the city's affordable housing program
guidelines established in section 4.79.030 , the following fees shall be eligible for subsidy, for those projects that construct
affordable housing units in excess of the amounts required by section 15.05.220 .
1.
For projects that provide more than the minimum of 12 percent of the total units in
the development as affordable units:
a.
Sewer system development fees;
b.
Water system development fees;
c.
Windy Gap surcharge; and
d.
Electric community investment fee.
2.
For projects that provide a minimum of 25 percent of the total units in the development
as affordable units:
a.
Cash-in-lieu of water rights received.
(Code 1993, § 4.79.020; Code 2009, § 4.79.020; Ord. No. O-92-65, § 1; Ord. No. O-95-05,
§ 2; Ord. No. O-95-78, § 2; Ord. No. O-99-48, § 5; Ord. No. O-2002-17, § 2; Ord. No.
O-2003-43, § 4; Ord. No. O-2004-43, § 2; Ord. No. O-2017-44, § 2, 9-12-2017; Ord.
No. O-2018-51 , § 3, 12-11-2018; Ord. No. O-2021-19 , § 3, 4-13-2021)
A.
Each person or organization may apply for fee reductions or subsidies, along with
a building permit application, using the forms supplied by the city, available through
the city's housing and community investment office. The planning director shall base
the amount of fee reduction or subsidy upon the following factors:
1.
For applicants requesting fee reductions or subsidies for constructing affordable
housing:
a.
The number of units to be occupied by tenants or purchased and occupied by homeowners
whose incomes meet the specific percentages of the median income by occupancy type
as set by sections 15.04.030.A.2 and 15.10.020 ;
b.
Whether or not there is a commitment by the applicant to provide permanently affordable
housing;
c.
Whether or not there is a commitment by the applicant to provide a minimum of five
percent of the total units to one or more special needs populations including, but
not limited to, large family (four or more bedrooms), homeless, elderly, physically
disabled and agricultural workers;
d.
Whether there is a commitment by the applicant to giving a priority to households
on the city housing authority waiting list for low rent housing;
e.
Whether or not there are construction features that lower the cost of housing for
low income consumers;
f.
The number and type of affordable housing units to be constructed in excess of the
minimum requirements of this Code; and
g.
Any other factors consistent with the intent of this chapter that the city manager
or designee may from time to time set forth in an administrative regulation.
2.
For applicants requesting fee reductions or subsidies for constructing attainable
housing:
a.
The number of units to be sold at a price below the median sales price in the City
of Longmont for a given unit type, as published by the City of Longmont to individual
homeowners whose incomes meet the specific percentages of the median income by occupancy
type as set by subsection C of this section.
b.
Whether or not there is a commitment by the applicant to provide deed restrictions
on the attainable housing for a period of time as determined by the city manager or
designee. Such deed restrictions shall contain all terms determined by the city manager
or designee to be appropriate to ensure the affordability of the unit and compliance
with this section. The council may consider allowing an owner of an attainable unit
to sell to a buyer who does not meet the qualifications of subsection C of this section
in exceptional circumstances involving significant disruption to the local economy
or individual financial hardship. In exchange, the owner would transfer equity to
the city at that time. The city manager or designee may specify in the deed restriction
the amount of such equity. The amount shall be revised upon each sale of the attainable
unit and shall be based on the difference between the most recent attainable sales
price and the estimated market price of the unit at the time of most recent sale.
c.
Whether or not there is a commitment by the applicant to grant employees a preference
to purchase a percentage of units that is proportional to the percentage of employer
invested funds in the development.
d.
Whether or not there is a commitment by the applicant to grant preference to individuals
employed or relocating to Longmont for employment.
e.
Whether or not there is a commitment by the applicant to enter into a development
agreement with the city, the terms of which shall be negotiated at the discretion
of the city manager to further the housing goals of the city. Satisfaction of this
criteria shall be a requirement to be eligible for consideration of fee waivers greater
than 45 percent.
f.
Whether or not the project provides an exceptional benefit to the community consistent
with the Envision Longmont Comprehensive Plan goals.
g.
Any other factors consistent with the intent of this chapter that the city manager
or designee may from time to time set forth in an administrative regulation.
h.
An applicant who has made a commitment to provide a minimum of 25 percent of the total
units in the development as affordable units with the remaining units in the project
as attainable housing may propose alternatives to the strict application of any administrative
regulations and program guidelines implementing this section. Such a proposal need
not meet the otherwise applicable substantive requirements of this section, but must
be approved by the city council.
B.
The application shall contain the name and address of each property owner, the developer,
a description of the project and such supporting information deemed necessary by the
city manager or designee.
C.
For the purposes of this chapter, "attainable housing" shall be defined as follows:
1.
Attainable owner housing means a dwelling unit sold for occupancy to a homeowner whose
income is between 80.1 percent and 120 percent of the area median income and sold
so as to be affordable to homeowner whose income is between 80.1 percent and 120 percent
of the area median income.
(Code 1993, § 4.79.030; Code 2009, § 4.79.030; Ord. No. O-92-65, § 1; Ord. No. O-95-05,
§ 2; Ord. No. O-95-78, § 3; Ord. No. O-97-08, § 2; Ord. No. O-99-48, § 6; Ord. No.
O-2002-17, § 3; Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 9, 8-14-2018; Ord. No. O-2021-19 , § 4, 4-13-2021; Ord. No. O-2023-59 , § 3, 11-14-2023)
Any decision by the planning director under this chapter or any applicable administrative
regulation related to this chapter may be appealed to the city manager. The appeal
shall be made in writing no later than ten days after the decision of the planning
director.
(Code 1993, § 4.79.040; Code 2009, § 4.79.040; Ord. No. O-92-65, § 1; Ord. No. O-95-05,
§ 2; Ord. No. O-2018-31 , § 9, 8-14-2018; Ord. No. O-2021-19 , § 5, 4-13-2021)
An applicant wanting a fee subsidy or reduction in addition to those in section 4.79.020 must submit a written application to the council. The council may approve the additional
fee reduction or subsidy if the applicant proves compliance with section 4.79.030 , a need for the housing exists, and provides adequate financial information showing
the project is economically unfeasible without the fee reduction or subsidy.
(Code 1993, § 4.79.045; Code 2009, § 4.79.045; Ord. No. O-95-78, § 4; Ord. No. O-99-47,
§ 3)
All reductions or subsidies granted by this chapter shall be reduced to a written
agreement between all the owners of the property and the city. The written agreement
shall be subject to such terms and conditions of this chapter and shall guarantee
performance or satisfaction of the eligibility criteria and other pertinent development
conditions. The written agreement shall run with the land and be binding upon the
heirs and successors of the owners until such time that the agreement expires. The
written agreement shall require that all owners and developers of the property be
responsible for the performance and satisfaction of its terms and conditions. If the
owners or developers do not perform or satisfy any term or condition of the agreement,
the city council may revoke the grant of any reductions or subsidies, and the property
and the owners and developers of the property shall be subject to all the fees and
requirements that would, but for the reductions or subsidies of this chapter, have
been applied.
(Code 1993, § 4.79.050; Code 2009, § 4.79.050; Ord. No. O-92-65, § 1; Ord. No. O-95-05,
§ 2; Ord. No. O-99-47, § 4)
The city manager shall report to the city council on the costs and benefits of this
chapter by December 31 of each year.
(Code 1993, § 4.79.060; Code 2009, § 4.79.060; Ord. No. O-92-65, § 1; Ord. No. O-95-05,
§ 2)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the flexible spending plan fund, for the purpose of the
deposit of funds voluntarily withheld from employee salaries for eligible reimbursement
programs.
(Code 1993, § 4.80.010; Code 2009, § 4.80.010; Ord. No. O-90-48, § 1)
Pursuant to the authority of section 9.9 of the Charter there is established a special fund designed as the Harvest Junction
East Special Revenue Fund, for the purpose of identifying, accounting for, and controlling
all revenues and expenses attributable to the following activities: receipt and expenditure
of interfund loan proceeds; collection of special revenue for the payment of principal
and interest; and additional related expenses, if any, on the interfund loan.
(Ord. No. O-20202-63, § 1(4.81.010), 12-1-2020)
Unless city council determines otherwise, this fund shall be funded by a portion of
the sales tax revenues derived from the retail operations at the Harvest Junction
East development, and any additional revenue as determined by city council.
(Ord. No. O-20202-63, § 1(4.81.020), 12-1-2020)
Payment from the fund shall be for Harvest Junction East development public improvements,
site development costs, system development fees and incidentals, Harvest Junction
East property purchases, and payment of principal and interest, and additional related
expenses, if any, on the interfund loan.
(Ord. No. O-20202-63, § 1(4.81.030), 12-1-2020)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter and C.R.S.
§ 39-22-514, known as the historic preservation fund, for the purpose of the deposit
of funds received from applications for the state income tax credit for rehabilitations
to qualified historic properties. The monies in the historic preservation fund will
only be used for the purposes of carrying out the duties of administering the state
income tax credit program and other related historic preservation functions.
(Code 1993, § 4.82.010; Code 2009, § 4.82.010; Ord. No. O-91-12, § 1)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the sustainability fund, for the purpose of funding and
implementing sustainability initiatives.
(Code 2009, § 4.83.010; Ord. No. O-2019-57 , § 1, 10-22-2019)
The sustainability fund shall be funded by all revenues designated by the city council,
including grants and intergovernmental revenues, intended to support sustainability
initiatives.
(Code 2009, § 4.83.020; Ord. No. O-2019-57 , § 1, 10-22-2019)
No revenues segregated, credited and deposited in special funds under chapters 4.32 , 4.44 , 4.48 , 4.52 , 4.60 or 4.64 shall be transferred to any other funds of the city, except to pay expenses attributable
to the respective fund purposes. No revenues deposited in such special funds shall
be used for general or other governmental or proprietary purposes of the city, except
to pay for an equitable share of the costs of equipment, materials, construction,
facilities or services, including accounting and management, provided by other funds.
(Code 1993, § 4.85.010; Code 2009, § 4.85.010; Ord. No. O-92-75, § 3)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the Jones Foundation Expendable Trust Fund, for the purpose
of the deposit of funds received from the Roger M. Jones and Roberta W. Jones Foundation.
Such funds are to be used for improving the Izaak Walton Pond area and the greenway
along St. Vrain Creek.
(Code 1993, § 4.86.010; Code 2009, § 4.86.010; Ord. No. O-92-41, § 1)
There shall be created a special fund, pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the community enhancement nonexpendable trust fund. The
purpose of the fund is to receive and use nonexpendable trust funds from various benefactors.
After the council accepts such funds, and any respective terms and conditions, the
city manager shall use them for community enhancement according to the respective
conditions stated by each benefactor and accepted by the council. The city manager
or designee shall establish and maintain reasonable and appropriate accounts, controls
and records to fulfill the purposes of the respective trusts.
(Code 1993, § 4.87.010; Code 2009, § 4.87.010; Ord. No. O-99-45, § 1)
The fund balances of the city's governmental fund types and fiduciary fund types are
considered to be reserves of those funds. The working capital of its proprietary fund
types is considered to be reserves of those funds.
(Code 1993, § 4.88.010; Code 2009, § 4.88.010; Ord. No. O-92-71, § 1)
Article X, section 20 of the state constitution requires the establishment of emergency
reserves to be used only for declared emergencies in accordance with such section
of the constitution. Such reserves will be titled "reserved for emergencies" in the
fund equity of the city's funds.
(Code 1993, § 4.88.020; Code 2009, § 4.88.020; Ord. No. O-92-71, § 1)
The required emergency reserves shall be maintained at an amount as required by the
state constitution in the general fund.
(Code 1993, § 4.88.030; Code 2009, § 4.88.030; Ord. No. O-92-71, § 1; Ord. No. O-94-06,
§ 1)
The water system construction reserve fund is created per section 9.9 of the Charter to make improvements to the city's water enterprise system related
to growth of the entire system. The fund shall be funded with applicable development
fees.
(Code 1993, § 4.90.010; Code 2009, § 4.90.010; Ord. No. O-94-22, § 1)
The water bond improvements fund is created per section 9.9 of the Charter to make a variety of improvements to the city's water enterprise system
to be financed from sales of bonds. The fund will be funded from bond proceeds as
they are sold.
(Code 1993, § 4.91.010; Code 2009, § 4.91.010; Ord. No. O-94-22, § 2)
The water acquisition fund is created per section 9.9 of the Charter for the purpose of acquiring additional water rights for the city
as needed to support its water enterprise system and for conservation programs that
offset the need to purchase additional water rights. The fund shall be funded with
the cash payments received in lieu of water rights.
(Code 1993, § 4.92.010; Code 2009, § 4.92.010; Ord. No. O-94-22, § 3; Ord. No. O-2004-62,
§ 1)
Editor's note— Ord. No. O-2022-51 , § 3, adopted Nov. 29, 2022, repealed Ch. 4.93, § 4.93.010 entitled "Raw Water Storage
Reserve Fund," which derived from: Code 1993, § 4.93.010; Code 2009, § 4.93.010; and
Ord. No. O-94-22, § 4.
Pursuant to the authority of section 9.9 of the Charter there is established a special fund designed as the utility billing
customer information system fund, for the purpose of affecting centralized control
and accounting over the acquisition, maintenance and operations of the customer information
system, including connection, disconnection, billing and payment of utility services.
(Code 2009, § 4.94.010; Ord. No. O-2018-47 , § 1, 11-13-2018)
Unless city council determines otherwise, this fund shall be funded by contributions
from the utility funds which services are billed through the customer information
system, and any additional revenue as determined by city council.
(Code 2009, § 4.94.020; Ord. No. O-2018-47 , § 1, 11-13-2018)
The annual budget for each department and division thereof using the customer information
system shall designate, as a specific line item, the projected cost of customer information
system usage applicable to it, said allocated money to be transferred to the utility
billing customer information system fund.
(Code 2009, § 4.94.030; Ord. No. O-2018-47 , § 1, 11-13-2018)
Pursuant to the authority of section 9.9 of the Charter there is established a special fund designated as the Village at the
Peaks COP Special Revenue Fund, for the purpose of identifying, accounting for and
controlling all revenues and expenses attributable to the following activities: certificate
of participation issuance, expenditures of certificates of participation funds, collection
of special revenue for the payment of base rentals and additional rentals, if any,
on certificates of participation.
(Code 2009, § 4.95.010; Ord. No. O-2014-48, § 1, 9-9-2014)
Unless council determines otherwise, this fund shall be funded by the revenues derived
from property tax increment revenue from the tax increment financing district within
the Twin Peaks Mall Urban Renewal Area, sales tax increment revenue from the tax increment
financing district within the Twin Peaks Mall Urban Renewal Area, Twin Peaks Metro
District levy and specific ownership tax revenue, and any addition revenue as determined
by city council.
(Code 2009, § 4.95.020; Ord. No. O-2014-48, § 1, 9-9-2014)
Payment from the fund shall be for certificate of participation issuance and financing
costs, expenditures of certificate of participation funds, payment of base rentals
and additional rentals, if any, on certificates of participation and any other lawful
expenditure authorized by city council.
(Code 2009, § 4.95.030; Ord. No. O-2014-48, § 1, 9-9-2014)
The sewer construction fund is created per section 9.9 of the Charter for the purpose of making improvements to the city's sewer enterprise
system related to growth of the entire system. The fund shall be funded with applicable
development fees.
(Code 1993, § 4.96.010; Code 2009, § 4.96.010; Ord. No. O-94-22, § 5)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the other post-employment benefits trust fund, for the purpose
of segregating funds for city expenses incurred under the city other post-employment
benefits trust fund.
(Code 2009, § 4.97.010; Ord. No. O-2010-48, § 1, 10-26-2010)
The OPEB trust fund shall be funded through city contributions determined by the city
manager for any year in which benefits are offered through the city's blended medical
benefit premiums for participating retirees. The city manager shall annually, in conjunction
with the budget process of the city, revise the city contributions as the city manager
determines.
(Code 2009, § 4.97.020; Ord. No. O-2010-48, § 1, 10-26-2010)
Payments from the fund shall be limited to claims or related administrative costs
for qualifying expenses of the city OPEB trust.
(Code 2009, § 4.97.030; Ord. No. O-2010-48, § 1, 10-26-2010)
Editor's note— Formerly entitled "Telecommunications Utility Enterprise Fund," renamed by Ord. No.
O-2013-37.
There is created a special fund, pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the electric and broadband utility enterprise fund, for
the purpose of identifying and controlling all revenues and expenses attributable
to municipal electric and broadband services.
(Code 1993, § 4.98.010; Code 2009, § 4.98.010; Ord. No. O-97-06, § 3; Ord. No. O-2013-37,
§ 2, 8-27-2013)
The electric and broadband utility enterprise fund shall be funded through revenues
derived from collection of electric and broadband utility related fees, charges, lease
payments and rentals, and appropriations from the general fund or other fund, as the
council may from time to time establish or approve.
(Code 1993, § 4.98.020; Code 2009, § 4.98.020; Ord. No. O-97-06, § 3; Ord. No. O-2013-37,
§ 2, 8-27-2013)
There is created a special fund, pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the affordable housing fund, for the purpose of receiving
all revenues related to affordable housing programs and services. The fund and any
earned interest shall be for the specific use of those programs and services.
(Code 1993, § 4.99.010; Code 2009, § 4.99.010; Ord. No. O-2001-68, § 2)
The affordable housing fund shall be funded through revenues derived from the special
retail marijuana sales tax as described in section 4.04.135 , collection of fees in lieu of construction of affordable housing or related to the
impacts thereon or other revenues collected under section 15.05.220 , from repayments of funds loaned from this fund, from gifts or grants, and from appropriations.
(Code 1993, § 4.99.020; Code 2009, § 4.99.020; Ord. No. O-2001-68, § 2; Ord. No. O-2017-47,
§ 3, 8-22-2017; Ord. No. O-2018-51 , § 4, 12-11-2018)
There is created a special fund, pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the attainable housing fund, for the purpose of receiving
all revenues related to attainable housing programs and services. The fund and any
earned interest shall be for the specific purpose of providing attainable housing
programs and services that serve people earning between 80.1—120 percent of the area
median income (AMI).
(Ord. No. O-2022-39 , § 1, 10-25-2022)
The attainable housing fund shall be funded by grants and intergovernmental revenues
received for the purpose defined above, from repayments of funds loaned from this
fund, as well as city funds intended to support attainable housing initiatives.
(Ord. No. O-2022-39 , § 1, 10-25-2022)
Pursuant to the authority of Article 9.9 , Municipal Charter of Longmont, there is established a special fund designated as
the state and local fiscal recovery fund, for the purpose of deposit of federal funding,
pursuant to the American Rescue Plan Act (31 CFR Part 35), and the disbursal of moneys
therefrom in accordance with the stated purposes and eligible uses, and to otherwise
achieve compliance with all applicable federal laws and regulations.
(Ord. No. O-2022-33 , § 1, 8-23-2022)
This fund shall be funded by federal funding received pursuant to the American Rescue
Plan Act (31 CFR Part 35).
(Ord. No. O-2022-33 , § 1, 8-23-2022)
Payment from the fund shall be for the stated purposes and eligible uses set forth
in the American Rescue Plan Act (31 CFR Part 35), including responding to the far-reaching
public health and negative economic impacts of the Covid 19 pandemic, and to otherwise
achieve compliance with all applicable federal laws and regulations.
(Ord. No. O-2022-33 , § 1, 8-23-2022)
Pursuant to the authority of article 9.9 of the Municipal Charter of Longmont, there is established a special fund designated
as the 1st and Main transit facilities fund, for the purposes of receiving revenues
from the Regional Transportation District for Longmont transit facilities. The fund,
along with any related city funding and any earned interest, shall be for the specific
purpose of land acquisition, planning and construction of the transit facilities.
(Ord. No. O-2024-31 , § 1, 5-28-2024)
The 1st and Main transit facilities fund shall be funded by intergovernmental revenues
received for the purposes defined above, as well as city funds intended to support
acquisition, planning and construction of the transit facilities.
(Ord. No. O-2024-31 , § 1, 5-28-2024)
There is created a special fund pursuant to section 9.9 of the Charter, known as the information technology service fund, for the purpose
of affecting centralized control over the acquisition, maintenance, allocation and
disposal of all city-wide information technology resources.
( Ord. No. O-2025-67 , § 1, 10-28-2025)
Unless city council determines otherwise, this fund shall be funded through the establishment
of an information technology charge received from the other city funds which benefit
from city-wide information technology resources, and any additional revenue as determined
by city council.
( Ord. No. O-2025-67 , § 1, 10-28-2025)
The annual budget for each department and division thereof using the information technology
resources shall designate, as a specific line item, the projected cost of information
technology resources usage applicable to it, said allocated money to be transferred
to the information technology service fund.
( Ord. No. O-2025-67 , § 1, 10-28-2025)
All city-wide information technology resources may be designated as an asset of the
information technology service fund.
( Ord. No. O-2025-67 , § 1, 10-28-2025)
